and rank is subadditive over formal sums. The bound refers to a chosen presentation of the network.

*Proof.* Write $V_{\mathcal I},V_{\mathcal O}$ for the input legs attached to $\mathcal I$ and $\mathcal O$, $U_{\mathcal I},U_{\mathcal O}$ likewise for the outputs, and $C$ for the tensor product of the cut wires. Contracting each side gives $T_{\mathcal I}:V_{\mathcal I}\to U_{\mathcal I}\otimes C$ and $T_{\mathcal O}:C\otimes V_{\mathcal O}\to U_{\mathcal O}$. Then $[\![\Gamma]\!]=(\mathrm{id}_{U_{\mathcal I}}\otimes T_{\mathcal O})\circ(T_{\mathcal I}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{V_{\mathcal O}})$ factors through $U_{\mathcal I}\otimes C\otimes V_{\mathcal O}$, of dimension $w$. Symmetric monoidal structure suffices; no cups or caps are needed. Subadditivity is $\operatorname{rank}(X+Y)\le\operatorname{rank}X+\operatorname{rank}Y$. ∎ [Num: no violation in 283 random networks.]

*Consequences.* For one box:
- the LoRA-family box $BA$: $\le r$; relative to $W_0$, splitting initialisations give $\Delta W=BA-B_0A_0$ of rank $\le2r$;
- HRA $\le r$, and a Givens circuit with $g$ gates $\le2g$, using the telescoping presentation $R-I=\sum_k(H_k-I)H_{k+1}\cdots H_r$ and subadditivity (naive expansion gives $2^r-1$ terms);
- ReLoRA after $K$ phases $\le Kr$; GraLoRA $\le kr$; LoHa $\le r_1r_2$;
- HiRA $\le r\operatorname{rk}W_0$, after factoring $W_0$ through a bond of dimension $\operatorname{rk}W_0$;
- LoKr $\le\operatorname{rk}C\cdot r$; FourierFT with $n_c$ coefficients $\le\min(2n_c,m,n)$;
- MoRA (reshape), C3A and MPO adapters up to full rank.

A large min-cut is necessary, not sufficient, for high rank. The "high-rank PEFT" literature consists of networks whose min-cut avoids an $r$-dimensional bottleneck; whether the bound is attained is a separate question.

**Theorem II.11 (Kronecker incomparability).** [Thm; linear algebra standard (Van Loan–Pitsianis, operator-Schmidt decomposition); PEFT reading new] Write $\mathbb R^m=\mathbb R^{m_1}\otimes\mathbb R^{m_2}$ and $\mathbb R^n=\mathbb R^{n_1}\otimes\mathbb R^{n_2}$, and view $\Delta\in\mathbb R^{m\times n}$ as a 4-leg tensor. Let $\operatorname{rk}$ be the rank across the cut $\{U_1U_2\}\mid\{V_1V_2\}$ and $\operatorname{rk}_\otimes$ the rank across $\{U_1V_1\}\mid\{U_2V_2\}$.

(a) $\operatorname{rk}_\otimes\Delta\le k$ iff $\Delta=\sum_{i\le k}C_i\otimes D_i$. Every $\Delta$ has $\operatorname{rk}_\otimes\Delta\le\min(m_1n_1,m_2n_2)$.

(b) $\operatorname{rk}(C\otimes D)=\operatorname{rk}C\cdot\operatorname{rk}D$, while $\operatorname{rk}_\otimes(C\otimes D)=1$ for $C,D\ne0$.

(c) If $u\in\mathbb R^m$ and $v\in\mathbb R^n$ have Schmidt ranks $\rho_u,\rho_v$, then $\operatorname{rk}_\otimes(uv^\top)=\rho_u\rho_v$. A generic rank-one update therefore has Kronecker rank $\kappa_1:=\min(m_1,m_2)\cdot\min(n_1,n_2)$. For aligned splits, $(m_1-m_2)(n_1-n_2)\ge0$, which include equal square splits and every split HF PEFT's factorisation produces (it puts the smaller factor first on both sides), $\kappa_1=\min(m_1n_1,m_2n_2)$ is the maximal Kronecker rank.

(d) **Incomparability.** Put $\nu:=\min(m_1,n_1)\min(m_2,n_2)$, the largest rank of a single Kronecker product. For $1\le r<\nu$ and $1\le k<\kappa_1$, the sets $\mathcal M_{\le r}$ and $\mathcal K_{\le k}:=\{\Delta:\operatorname{rk}_\otimes\Delta\le k\}$ of unconstrained $k$-term Kronecker sums are incomparable, for every split: a generic rank-one update lies in the first and not the second, and $C\otimes D$ with full-rank factors, of Kronecker rank $1$ and rank $\nu$, lies in the second and not the first. For equal square splits ($m=n=N$, all factors $s$), $\kappa_1=\nu=s^2=N$, and $I_N$ is such a product.

(e) **Constrained Kronecker methods.** Let $\mathrm{Im}\,M\subseteq W_0+\mathcal K_{\le k}$. Then $\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_r\not\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,M$ whenever $k<\kappa_1$, for every pointing of $\mathrm{LoRA}_r$. Give $\mathrm{LoRA}_r$ its zero-init pointing, with image $W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}$. Then $\mathrm{Im}\,M\not\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_r$ iff some $\theta\in\mathrm{Im}\,M$ has $\operatorname{rank}(\theta-W_0)>r$. For LoKr, $C\otimes BA$ with $C\in\mathbb R^{m_1\times n_1}$, $B\in\mathbb R^{m_2\times r'}$ and $A\in\mathbb R^{r'\times n_2}$, put $r'_{\rm eff}=\min(r',m_2,n_2)$. The maximum rank is $\min(m_1,n_1)\cdot r'_{\rm eff}$. So $\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoKr}_{r'}\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_r$ iff $r\ge\min(m_1,n_1)r'_{\rm eff}$, and otherwise the two images are incomparable, provided $\kappa_1>1$. In HF PEFT the second LoKr factor is low-rank only when $r<\max(m_2,n_2)/2$; otherwise it is full and LoKr is unconstrained KronA, with maximum rank $\min(m_1,n_1)\min(m_2,n_2)$. On a $768$-wide layer ($24\times32$) the maximum rank therefore jumps from $360$ at $r=15$ to $768$ at $r=16$. With `decompose_both`, $C$ is also capped at rank $r$.

*Proof.* (a) and (b) follow from the Van Loan–Pitsianis rearrangement $\mathcal R(C\otimes D)=\mathrm{vec}C\,\mathrm{vec}D^\top$, which is wire-bending; $\mathcal R\Delta$ is an $(m_1n_1)\times(m_2n_2)$ matrix. For (c), write the Schmidt decompositions $u=\sum\sigma_ix_i\otimes y_i$ and $v=\sum\tau_jz_j\otimes w_j$. Then $uv^\top=\sum_{ij}\sigma_i\tau_j(x_iz_j^\top)\otimes(y_iw_j^\top)$ is an operator-Schmidt decomposition with Frobenius-orthonormal families and $\rho_u\rho_v$ nonzero coefficients; generic $u,v$ have full Schmidt ranks. Under alignment $\min(m_1,m_2)\min(n_1,n_2)=\min(m_1n_1,m_2n_2)$, which is the bound of (a). (d) follows from (b) and (c) and needs no alignment. (e) The first clause: if some pointing gave $c+\mathcal M_{\le r}\subseteq\mathcal K_{\le k}$, then for $X\in\mathcal M_{\le r}$ and $t>0$, $(c+tX)/t\in\mathcal K_{\le k}$, and letting $t\to\infty$ in this closed cone gives $\mathcal M_{\le r}\subseteq\mathcal K_{\le k}$, contradicting (c). The second clause is the definition of $\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_r$. For LoKr, $\operatorname{rank}(C\otimes BA)=\operatorname{rk}C\cdot\operatorname{rk}(BA)\le\min(m_1,n_1)\,r'_{\rm eff}$ by (b), with equality for full-rank $C$ and $BA$ of rank $r'_{\rm eff}$; $\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_r\not\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoKr}$ because $\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoKr}\subseteq W_0+\mathcal K_{\le1}$ and $1<\kappa_1$. ∎ [Num: Schmidt ranks $(1,3)\to3$, $(2,3)\to6$, $(3,3)\to9$, each with ordinary rank $1$; for the $2\times4$ split of $\mathbb R^8$, $\operatorname{rk}_\otimes(uv^\top)=4$, the maximal Kronecker rank; LoKr with $s=4$, $r'=1$ never exceeds rank $4$; at $(m_1,m_2,n_1,n_2)=(2,8,8,2)$ with inner dimension $4$, LoKr never exceeds rank $4=\min(m_1,n_1)\,r'_{\rm eff}$.]

*What this explains.* Unconstrained Kronecker sums (KronA with full factors, sums of Kronecker terms) are **LoRA across a different cut** of the same 4-leg tensor, not "higher-rank LoRAs". With aligned splits, a generic rank-one update needs the maximal number of Kronecker terms. They also rest on a reshape that the network does not have. Constrained Kronecker methods behave differently: LoKr with inner rank $r'$ sits inside a LoRA image only when the LoRA rank is at least $\min(m_1,n_1)r'$, about $\sqrt N\,r'$ (192 on 768-wide and 512 on 4096-wide layers at HF's default $r'=8$). At practical ranks, and at equal parameter budget, LoKr and $\mathrm{LoRA}_r$ are incomparable (e). Compacter's PHM weights are Kronecker-structured weights of a nonlinear bottleneck adapter, not an update of $W_0$, and MPO adapters are a multi-core reparametrisation that is lossless at full bond dimension; the image comparison does not transfer to either without more work. **[Pred]** On targets $W_0+uv^\top$ with generic $u,v$, and a loss whose only minimiser is the target (for instance regression on full-rank inputs), LoRA$_1$ reaches zero loss, while no Kronecker sum with fewer than $\kappa_1$ terms does. HF PEFT's balanced factorisation is non-square for many widths ($768\to24\times32$ with $\kappa_1=576$, the maximal Kronecker rank; $2048\to32\times64$; $5120\to64\times80$) but square for $1024$ and $4096$ ($32\times32$, $64\times64$), where $\kappa_1=N$. At an equalised budget neither method dominates the other.

**Proposition II.12 (gauge census: rank is a cut, dimension is a volume).** [Prop]

(a) $d(M)\le\lvert M\rvert-\dim(\text{generic gauge orbit})$, with equality when the gauge acts locally transitively on generic fibres.

(b) The following values hold for one $m\times n$ box, with $r\le\min(m,n)$ throughout except for VeRA (whose published RoBERTa-base setting has $r=1024>768$). The gauge column lists the exhibited Lie group; it is locally transitive on generic fibres away from the $mn$ cap, but not where the cap binds (for LoHa at $(4,4,2)$ the orbit has dimension $15$ and the fibre $16$).

| method | budget $\lvert M\rvert$ | exhibited Lie gauge (orbit dim) | $d(M)$ | max rank |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LoRA$_r$ | $r(m+n)$ | $GL_r$ ($r^2$) | $r(m+n-r)$ | $r$ |
| GraLoRA ($k\times k$ blocks, rank $r/k$ each; HF default `hybrid_r=0`) | $r(m+n)$ | $\prod GL_{r/k}$ ($r^2$) | $r(m+n-r)$ | $\min(kr,m,n)$ |
| LoHa$_{r_1,r_2}$ | $(r_1+r_2)(m+n)$ | $GL_{r_1}\times GL_{r_2}\times$ diagonal torus ($r_1^2+r_2^2+m+n-1$) | $\min\big(mn,\ r_1(m+n-r_1)+r_2(m+n-r_2)-(m+n-1)\big)$ [Num; the second argument is an upper bound] | $\min(r_1r_2,m,n)$ |
| HRA$_r$ ($\operatorname{rank}W_0=n$, `apply_GS=False`; even $r$ is needed only for HF's identity init) | $rn$ | non-linear; fibre dimension $r(r+1)/2$; exhibited Lie gauge $(\mathbb R^\times)^r$ | $rn-r(r+1)/2$ (for $r\in\{n-1,n\}$ this is $\dim SO(n)$ [Num]) | $r$ |
| DoRA$_r$ | $r(m+n)+m$ | $GL_r$ for generic $W_0$ with $(m-r)(n-r)\ge m$ (larger otherwise: dimension $6>r^2$ at $(3,3,2)$, and $r^2+r$ for rank-one $W_0$) | $\min\big(mn,\ r(m+n-r)+m\big)$ for generic $W_0$ (Prop II.8) | up to full |
| VeRA | $m+r$ | $\mathbb R^\times$ | $m+r-1$ [Num] | $\min(r,m,n)$ |
| KronA $A\otimes B$ | $m_1n_1+m_2n_2$ | $\mathbb R^\times$ | $m_1n_1+m_2n_2-1$ | $\min(m_1,n_1)\min(m_2,n_2)$ |
| TT / Tucker (admissible, minimal ranks) | $\sum$ cores | $\prod_eGL_{r_e}$ | $\lvert M\rvert-\sum_er_e^2$ (Tucker: Koch–Lubich, Uschmajew–Vandereycken; TT: Holtz–Rohwedder–Schneider) | up to full |
| linear methods (LoRA-FA, LoRA-XS, MiSS in every HF mode, FourierFT, C3A, masks) | $\lvert M\rvert$ | translations by the kernel | rank of the linear map; $=\lvert M\rvert$ when injective | varies |

BitFit does not touch the $m\times n$ box; it is linear in the biases. Hybrid GraLoRA (`hybrid_r>0`) is not covered by the table.

FourierFT is injective only when its sampled frequencies contain no conjugate pair $(u,v),(-u,-v)$; otherwise $d=\lvert M\rvert-\#\{\text{pairs}\}$. At LLM sizes this is close to $\lvert M\rvert$, but at $768\times768$ with $1000$ frequencies a pair occurs in about half of the draws (HF's default seed $777$ gives exactly two pairs there).

(c) **Equal-budget trades.** Assume $2r\le\min(m,n)$, $r^2\le\min(m,n)$ and $2r^2<m+n-1$. LoHa$_{r,r}$ and LoRA$_{2r}$ both cost $2r(m+n)$, but LoHa reaches at least $(m+n-1)-2r^2$ *fewer* dimensions (exactly that many when the LoHa upper bound of (b) is attained [Num]). For $r\ge3$ it gains rank up to $r^2>2r$ in exchange. For $r\le2$, $r^2\le2r$, so $\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoHa}_{r,r}\subsetneq\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_{2r}$: LoHa is strictly dominated. When $2r^2\ge m+n-1$ (still with $2r\le\min(m,n)$) the bound no longer separates them, and wherever the LoHa upper bound of (b) is attained LoHa$_{r,r}$ matches or exceeds LoRA$_{2r}$ in dimension as well as in rank, for instance at $(m,n,r)=(9,9,3)$, at $r=64$ on $4096\times4096$, and at LyCORIS-style $r=32$ on $320$- to $640$-wide layers. GraLoRA$_k$ costs the same as LoRA$_r$, reaches the same number of dimensions, and has maximum rank $\min(kr,m,n)$, but caps every block at rank $r/k$. A generic rank-$r$ update has rank $r$ on every block, so for $k\ge2$ and $r\le\min(m,n)/k$ the images of LoRA$_r$ and GraLoRA$_k$ are incomparable.

*Proof.* (a) The generic fibre contains the generic gauge orbit, and $d=\lvert M\rvert-\dim(\text{generic fibre})$.

The LoHa torus acts by $(B_1,A_1,B_2,A_2)\mapsto(D_1B_1,A_1D_2,D_1^{-1}B_2,A_2D_2^{-1})$. It preserves the product because $(D_1XD_2)\odot(D_1^{-1}YD_2^{-1})=X\odot Y$. At generic parameters, the joint action of $GL_{r_1}\times GL_{r_2}\times T_m\times T_n$ has a one-dimensional stabiliser, $\{(cI,c^{-1}I,cI,c^{-1}I)\}$: if $D_1B_1=B_1g_1$ for generic $B_1$ with $m>r_1$, every row of $B_1$ is a left eigenvector of $g_1$, so $g_1=cI$ and $D_1=cI$, and the other three equations force $D_2=c^{-1}I$ and $g_2=c^{-1}I$. So the orbit has dimension $r_1^2+r_2^2+m+n-1$, which gives the upper bound by (a). Trivially $d\le mn$.

GraLoRA's image is a product of $k^2$ cones $\mathcal M_{\le r/k}(\tfrac mk\times\tfrac nk)$, of total dimension $k^2\cdot\tfrac rk\cdot\tfrac{m+n-r}{k}=r(m+n-r)$. For the rank, write block $(i,j)$ as $U_{ij}V_{ij}^\top$ with $U_{ij}\in\mathbb R^{m/k\times r/k}$ and $V_{ij}\in\mathbb R^{n/k\times r/k}$. Then $\Delta W=\mathrm{blockdiag}(L_1,\dots,L_k)\,S$, with $L_i=[U_{i1}\cdots U_{ik}]\in\mathbb R^{m/k\times r}$ and $S\in\mathbb R^{kr\times n}$ stacking the $V_{ij}^\top$, each supported on column block $j$. For generic blocks and $r\le\min(m,n)/k$, each $L_i$ has full column rank $r$ and $S$ has full row rank $kr$, so $\operatorname{rank}\Delta W=kr$. In general the rank is at most $\min(kr,m,n)$. (Placing rank-$r/k$ blocks only on the diagonal gives rank $r$, not $kr$.)

LoHa$_{r,r}$ maps into LoRA$_{r^2}$ (the face-splitting arrow of Exposé VIII.C), which gives domination for $r\le2$. Under $2r^2<m+n-1$ the LoHa upper bound is $2r(m+n-2r)-\big((m+n-1)-2r^2\big)<d(\mathrm{LoRA}_{2r})\le mn$, so the cap does not bind, the gap is at least $(m+n-1)-2r^2>0$, and strictness follows. Conversely, when $2r^2\ge m+n-1$ the bound no longer separates them. The KronA image is a Segre cone. HRA is Thm II.7. The remaining rows are standard; for FourierFT, $\mathrm{Re}\circ\mathrm{ifft2}$ sends the frequencies $p$ and $-p$ to the same real pattern. ∎ [Num: LoHa $38,59,93,101$ at $(m,n,r)=(8,7,2),(12,10,2),(12,10,3),(20,16,2)$, and $16=mn$ at $(4,4,2)$, where the formula gives $17$ and $d(\mathrm{LoRA}_4)=16$ too; LoHa$_{3,3}$ against LoRA$_6$: $73$ against $72$ at $(9,9,3)$; GraLoRA dimension $48,64,108$ at $(m,n,r,k)=(8,8,4,2),(12,8,4,2),(12,12,6,3)$; GraLoRA generic rank $8=kr$ at $(16,16,4,2)$ and $12=\min(m,n)$ at $(12,12,6,3)$.]

*What this explains.* Rank is a *cut* invariant and dimension a *volume* invariant, and the literature conflates them. When $r\ge3$ and $r^2\ll(m+n)/2$, LoHa reaches high rank on a thin, curved subvariety, because its Hadamard product spends a whole torus on gauge. When $2r^2\ge m+n-1$, as in LyCORIS's usual Stable Diffusion settings, it matches or exceeds LoRA$_{2r}$ in dimension too. GraLoRA keeps LoRA's dimension and multiplies the maximum rank by $k$, but caps every block at rank $r/k$: it moves the cut rather than winning for free. **[Pred]** Benchmarks should report $d(M)$ beside $\lvert M\rvert$; at budgets of a few thousand parameters, rankings at equal *effective* dimension can change.

**Proposition II.13 (transport of structure).** [Prop] For $T\in GL(\Theta)$ put $\tau_T(\theta)=\theta_0+T(\theta-\theta_0)$ and $T_*M:=(Q,q_0,\tau_T\circ\rho)$.
- $T_*$ is a strict automorphism of $\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)$, with inverse $(T^{-1})_*$. It maps $S_1$ to $T\,S_1$ and preserves $d$, $g$, the gauge group, the first-order deficit, polynomial degree, the regular or apex type and the germ type of the image. It does not preserve rank, and, for the family extension below, it does not preserve the covariance group.
- $K^{T_*M}=T\,K^M\,T^\top$ for every $T$. For any update rule that queries the loss only through $q\mapsto L(\rho(q))$, with its own state on $Q$, $T_*M$ trained on $L$ and $M$ trained on $L\circ\tau_T$ have identical $q$-trajectories, so their $\theta$-trajectories are conjugate by $\tau_T$. Rules that read $\nabla_\theta L$ and solve a least-squares problem on $\Theta$ (LoRA-Pro), and $\theta$-level weight decay or clipping, are covered only for orthogonal $T$. Orthogonality of $T$ is exactly the condition for $\tau_T$ to be a Frobenius isometry; it is needed only to keep the Frobenius spectrum of $K$, or an isometry-invariant loss class such as $\|\theta-\theta^\star\|^2$, unchanged.
- Coordinatewise optimizers (SGD, Adam) are also equivariant under coordinate permutations of $Q$. The Kronecker identification below needs this, because it composes $\mathcal R^{-1}_*$ with the vec reshape of $Q$.
- *Families and rebasing.* For $T$ independent of the base point, define the family extension $(T_*M)(\theta):=(Q,q_0,\theta+T(\rho_\theta-\theta))$. For an additive family with fixed shape $C$ (Def I.2), $T_*M$ is additive with shape $TC$, so transport commutes with rebasing: $R_K(T_*M)=\tau_T(R_K(M))$ (Thm V.3(a)). For action-type families this can fail even for $T=2I$, because the transported family acts by $I+T(g-I)$: for OFT on $\Theta=\mathbb R^{1\times2}$, two cycles of $\theta\mapsto\theta(2R-I)$ with $R=\mathrm{rot}(\pi)$ reach $9\theta_0$, outside the transported circle. Transports that depend on $\theta_0$ may fail (HiRA below) or may not (LoRA transported by the SVD frame of $\theta_0$, since $\mathcal M_{\le r}$ is $O(m)\times O(n)$-invariant). The covariance group (Def II.14) is a property of a family and is compared through this extension.

Instances:
- **Kronecker family.** An isomorphism of slices over $\Theta'=\mathbb R^{m_1n_1\times m_2n_2}$ and over $\Theta$: $\sum_{i\le k}C_i\otimes D_i=\mathcal R^{-1}_*\mathrm{LoRA}_k$, with $Q$ reshaped by vec, dynamics included for coordinatewise optimizers. KronA with unconstrained factors is $\mathcal R^{-1}_*\mathrm{LoRA}_1$. LoKr is $\mathcal R^{-1}_*$ of LoRA$_1$ with the second factor constrained to $\mathrm{vec}\,\mathcal M_{\le r'}$ when $r<\max(m_2,n_2)/2$ (HF's switch); otherwise HF's second factor is full and LoKr is KronA. LoKr's `decompose_both` option also constrains the first factor.
- **Linear methods as coordinate subspaces in transported frames.**
  - FourierFT: sparse in the real cosine half of the real Fourier frame. HF's $\Delta W=c\,\mathrm{Re}(\mathrm{ifft2}(S))$, with scaling $c$ (default $150$), is the transport of a mask, with $g=0$, only when the sampled index set has no conjugate pair $(u,v),(-u,-v)$; otherwise $g$ is the number of such pairs. Its image lies in the point-symmetric subspace $\Delta W_{j,l}=\Delta W_{-j,-l}$, and the frame is orthogonal only up to scale (self-conjugate frequencies have twice the squared norm).
  - WaveFT: sparse in a wavelet frame, when HF's pad–IDWT–crop map is invertible (plausible for Haar on even shapes; unverified for other wavelet families).
  - C3A: block-circulant. Over $\mathbb R$ the frame is given by the powers $P^k$ of the cyclic shift (Gram matrix $bI$). Over $\mathbb C$, $\mathrm{circ}(w)=F^{-1}\mathrm{diag}(Fw)F$ for first column $w$, and the real form has $2\times2$ rotation–scaling blocks. HF adds a factor $1/\mathrm{in\_features}$.
  - LoRA-XS, SVFT, TinyLoRA: masks or random subspaces in the SVD frame of $W_0$, a $\theta_0$-dependent transport.
  - SHiRA, FISH, Trainable Tokens: masks in the standard frame.
  - MiSS/Bone in its default (balance) mode: $\Delta W=D(\mathbf 1_g^\top\otimes I_r)$, which is LoRA-FA with a frozen *summing* projection. Its BAT mode, $\Delta W=\mathrm{blocks}(W_0)\,M+M$ blockwise, is linear in the trained block $M$ but depends on $\theta_0$, so it is not additive.
  - These frame readings of FourierFT and C3A are for `init_weights=True`. HF's defaults start off $\theta_0$: FourierFT draws its spectrum from $\mathcal N(0,1)$ with scaling $150$ (at $768^2$ with $1000$ frequencies, $\lVert\Delta W_0\rVert_F\approx4.3$, about $14\%$ of a std-$0.04$ $W_0$), and C3A is Xavier-uniform. These are objects with a pointing defect.
- **HiRA** $=(D_{W_0})_*\mathrm{LoRA}$ inside the slice at $\theta_0$, when $W_0$ has no zero entries. Because $D_{W_0}$ depends on $\theta_0$, re-based HiRA is not transported ReLoRA: two cycles reach $W_0\odot(J+Z_1)\odot(J+Z_2)$, whose relative update $Z_1+Z_2+Z_1\odot Z_2$ has rank up to $\min(2r+r^2,m,n)$, attained, not $\le2r$.

*Proof.* $D(\tau_T\circ\rho)=T\,D\rho$ gives the statements about $S_1$, the ranks of the differential, $d$, $g$ and the deficit, and $K^{T_*M}=TD\rho\Lambda D\rho^\top T^\top$. Since $T_*$ does not touch $Q$, $L\circ\rho_{T_*M}=(L\circ\tau_T)\circ\rho_M$ exactly. Coordinate permutations of $Q$ commute with every coordinatewise update rule. A rule reading $\nabla_\theta L$ through a Frobenius least-squares step moves by $-(D\rho^\top T^\top TD\rho)^+D\rho^\top T^\top\nabla L$ under $T_*M$, against $-(D\rho^\top D\rho)^+D\rho^\top T^\top\nabla L$ for $M$ on $L\circ\tau_T$; they agree when $T^\top T=I$. For additive families, $R_K$ is the $K$-fold Minkowski sum of the shape, and $T$ is linear, so $T(C^{+K})=(TC)^{+K}$ by induction. For OFT with $T=2I$, $\theta_0(2\,\mathrm{rot}(\pi)-I)^2=9\theta_0$. The circulant identity is the DFT diagonalisation. $\mathrm{Re}\circ\mathrm{ifft2}$ sends conjugate frequencies to the same real pattern. The MiSS formula is its definition in efficient form. For HiRA, $(J+Z_1)\odot(J+Z_2)=J+Z_1+Z_2+Z_1\odot Z_2$. ∎ [Num: $\|K^{T_*M}-TK^MT^\top\|=2\times10^{-14}$ for a Gaussian $T$; Adam on $T_*M$ with $L$ and on $M$ with $L\circ\tau_T$ agree to $8\times10^{-11}$; two HiRA cycles give relative-update rank $3$, $8$, $15$ at $r=1,2,3$ ($m=n=30$).]

*What this explains.* "High rank" claims (MoRA, HiRA, LoKr, C3A, SHiRA, WaveFT) are not $GL(\Theta)$-invariant, although rank is $GL_m\times GL_n$-invariant. The transports in play ($\mathcal R$, the real Fourier (cosine) frame, $D_{W_0}$) do not preserve the network function, and Thm II.11 shows that KronA and $\mathrm{LoRA}_r$ have incomparable images when $r<\min(m_1,n_1)\min(m_2,n_2)$ and $\kappa_1>1$. Transport preserves every isomorphism invariant of the method ($d$, $g$, the gauge group, $\dim S_1$, the deficit, the regular or apex type, the germ type and the degree) and conjugates its dynamics. Relative to the network's $GL_m\times GL_n$ structure it changes, in particular, rank, covariance, $S_1$ as a subspace, and the image itself. **[Pred]** At matched learning-rate sweeps, the performance gap between a transported method and its source (Kronecker sums against LoRA; Fourier or wavelet methods against coordinate masks) is explained by how well each image aligns with the task's update, measured for instance by projecting a full fine-tuning $\Delta W$ onto each image, rather than by optimizer effects.

**Definition II.14 (covariance group).** [Def] For a method defined for every $W_0$ (a natural family), the **covariance group** $\mathcal G_M\subseteq GL_m\times GL_n$ is the largest subgroup with $\mathrm{Im}\,M(gW_0h^{-1})=g\,\mathrm{Im}\,M(W_0)\,h^{-1}$. When the method depends on random frames or on data, the identity is required in distribution, with the data transformed. $\mathcal G_M$ is the *geometry the method lives in*, in Klein's sense.

**Observation II.15 (descent through function-preserving reparametrisations).** [Obs] Let $M=\prod_bM_b$ be a box-wise method: each $M_b$ is a member of a natural family with covariance group $\mathcal G_{M_b}$, and no trainable parameters or random frames are shared across boxes. Let $G_{\rm arch}$ act on $\Theta=\prod_b\Theta_b\times\Theta_{\rm frozen}$, preserving this split and acting on each adapted box by $W_b\mapsto g_bW_bh_b^{-1}$ with $(g_b,h_b)\in\mathcal G_{M_b}$. Assume $f(g\theta,-)=f(\theta,-)$ on a $G_{\rm arch}$-stable set containing $\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)$. Then $\mathrm{Im}\,M(g\theta_0)=g\cdot\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)$, and the set of *functions* reachable by $M$ from $g\theta_0$ equals the set reachable from $\theta_0$. For methods with random frames, the equality holds in distribution, provided the frames are drawn independently per box. Boxes that share a frame need a pathwise condition: a law-preserving map $T_{(g,h)}$ on frame space with $\mathrm{Im}\,M_b(g_bWh_b^{-1};TF)=g_b\,\mathrm{Im}\,M_b(W;F)\,h_b^{-1}$ for all $W$ and $F$, with the same $T$ on every box that shares $F$ (marginal covariance in law, as in Def II.14, is not enough). For data-dependent methods, require covariance pathwise in the data, and that the box-$b$ data at $g\theta_0$ is the Def II.14 transform under $(g_b,h_b)$ of the data at $\theta_0$. This holds for intertwiner gauges such as hidden-unit permutations, query–key and value–output head gauges and residual rotations. The statement concerns reachable sets only; training trajectories and outcomes can still differ, for instance under Adam or non-isotropic initialisations.

Examples of $G_{\rm arch}$:
- hidden-neuron permutations;
- the query–key gauge $(W_Q,W_K)\mapsto(gW_Q,g^{-\top}W_K)$ per head. With no positional encoding or an absolute one, $g\in GL_{d_k}$. Under RoPE, $g$ must commute with every RoPE rotation, which leaves the per-frequency $2\times2$ rotation–scalings $(\mathbb C^\times)^{d_k/2}$, shared across a GQA group. Under partial RoPE on $r$ of the $d_k$ dimensions the group is $(\mathbb C^\times)^{r/2}\times GL_{d_k-r}$. Under QK-norm, $g$ must in addition be orthogonal when the norm's $\varepsilon>0$ (conformal, $g\in CO(d_k)$, when $\varepsilon=0$) and satisfy $g^\top\Gamma_qR_t\Gamma_kg=\Gamma_qR_t\Gamma_k$ for every RoPE rotation $R_t$, where $\Gamma_q,\Gamma_k$ are the gains. OLMo 2 normalises $q$ and $k$ across all heads jointly;
- a single global orthogonal rotation $Q$ of the residual stream of a pre-norm transformer whose norm gains can all be fused into the linear layers they feed, after LayerNorm is converted to RMSNorm (SliceGPT's computational invariance; QuaRot's offline rotation). SliceGPT's per-block rotations $Q_\ell$ need extra residual matrices and change the architecture. Sandwich and post-sublayer norms (Gemma 2 and 3, OLMo 2) are excluded, because their gains cannot be fused and $\mathrm{diag}(\gamma)$ does not commute with $Q$. The comparison is between the gain-fused model and the fused-and-rotated model: gain fusion is a diagonal input rescaling outside $G_{\rm arch}$ that already changes PiSSA's split and block OFT's image (Prop II.16), and converting LayerNorm to RMSNorm changes LoRA's reachable set on the residual writers.

*Proof.* Apply per-box covariance factor by factor; the frozen coordinates are single points, so $\mathrm{Im}\,M(g\theta_0)=g\,\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)$. Then $\{\Phi_f(\theta):\theta\in\mathrm{Im}M(g\theta_0)\}=\{\Phi_f(g\theta):\theta\in\mathrm{Im}M(\theta_0)\}=\{\Phi_f(\theta):\theta\in\mathrm{Im}M(\theta_0)\}$. With independent per-box frames, the joint law is the product of the per-box laws, so per-box covariance in law gives the joint statement. With a shared frame, the pathwise condition applies the same $T$ to it in every box, and $T$ preserves its law. ∎ [Num: a tied rank-one update shared by two boxes violates the conclusion (relative distance up to $0.30$), while independent per-box LoRA satisfies it; a Haar frame shared by two boxes, each covariant in law, gives a joint statistic of $0.44$ from $\theta_0$ against $0.81$ from $g\theta_0$; under RoPE a generic $g\in GL_8$ changes attention scores by up to $313$, a RoPE-commuting $g$ by $5\times10^{-14}$.]

*What this explains.* Descent is decided by whether each induced pair $(g_b,h_b)$ lies in $\mathcal G_{M_b}$. For one-sided methods ((IA)$^3$, DoRA, OFT, BOFT) this is a question of side. For coordinate-structured methods (LoHa with $r\ge2$, HiRA, masks, FourierFT, Kronecker methods) it is a question of group: they descend through hidden-unit monomial gauges, since $P(X\odot Y)Q=(PXQ)\odot(PYQ)$, but not through rotations. A global residual rotation acts on the input side of $q$, $k$, $v$, gate and up, and on the output side of $o$ and down.
- *These satisfy the hypothesis*, so their reachable function sets are unchanged between the fused and the fused-and-rotated model: LoRA, full FT, BitFit, PiSSA; (IA)$^3$ with HF defaults, which scales the output side of $k$ (or $q$, for Qwen, Gemma and Phi) and $v$ and the input side of down, while the rotation acts on the other side, where (IA)$^3$ is $GL$-covariant; DoRA on $q$, $k$, $v$, gate and up; OFT on $o$ and down (no scale).
- *These fail the sufficient condition*: block OFT and BOFT on residual-input projections (the block normaliser contains no Hadamard or PCA rotation), BOFT on $o$ and down (its output scale `boft_s`), DoRA on $o$ and down, LoHa with $r\ge2$, HiRA, coordinate masks, FourierFT. (LoHa$_1$ has image $W_0+\mathcal M_{\le1}$ and satisfies it.)
- *Under query–key and value–output head gauges*, (IA)$^3$ on $q$ or $k$ and on $v$, and OFT on $o$, fail it.

Failing a sufficient condition does not prove that the reachable function sets differ. **[Conj A]** Against a gain-fused but unrotated control, fine-tuning the fused-and-rotated, function-identical model measurably changes the reachable function sets, and the results, of block OFT/BOFT on residual-input projections, LoHa, HiRA and coordinate masks, because LLM residual streams have a privileged basis. QuaRot's per-head Hadamard on $W_v$ and $W_{\rm out}$ is a value–output gauge in $G_{\rm arch}$. Its online Hadamards (across heads, before down_proj, on keys) preserve the realised function but insert new operations, so they change the architecture map and lie outside $G_{\rm arch}$. The optimizer adds a further layer: Adam is only $B_r$-equivariant (Thm III.12), so LoRA *trained with Adam* need not descend even though its image does.

**Proposition II.16 (covariant data-aware splits).** [Prop] Let $C=\mathbb E[xx^\top]\succ0$ be the input second moment, so that under $x\mapsto hx$ we have $W\mapsto Wh^{-1}$ and $C\mapsto hCh^\top$. Let $LL^\top=C$, write $[M]_r$ for the rank-$r$ truncated SVD, and $CO(n)=\mathbb R_{>0}\cdot O(n)$. Assume $1\le r<k:=\min(m,n)$ and the spectral gap each split needs: $\sigma_r>\sigma_{r+1}$ of $W_0L$ in (a); in (b), $\sigma_r>\sigma_{r+1}$ of $W_0C$ or $W_0$ for top-$r$ splits, and $\sigma_{k-r}>\sigma_{k-r+1}$ for bottom-$r$ splits. Covariance groups are taken over the generic set where these gaps hold.

(a) The whitened split $X^*=\arg\min_{\operatorname{rank}X\le r}\|(W_0-X)L\|_F=[W_0L]_rL^{-1}=\Pi_rW_0$, where $\Pi_r$ is the top-$r$ eigenprojector of the output second moment $W_0CW_0^\top$, is $GL_n$-covariant on the input side and $CO(m)$-covariant on the output side; its joint group is $CO(m)\times GL_n$. This is the classical reduced-rank-regression (SVD-LLM) solution.

(b) The formula-level CorDA splits, IPM ($X=[W_0C]_rC^{-1}$, the minimiser of $\|(W_0-X)C\|_F$) and KPM (the bottom-$r$ components of $W_0C$ mapped back by $C^{-1}$, $X=(W_0C-[W_0C]_{k-r})C^{-1}$), with or without CorDA's trace-relative damping $C+\kappa(\mathrm{tr}\,C/n)I$, and the PiSSA and MiLoRA splits ($[W_0]_r$ and the bottom-$r$ part $W_0-[W_0]_{k-r}$), have input-side covariance group exactly $CO(n)$; jointly, $CO(m)\times CO(n)$.

(c) **Scope.** An absolute damping $C+\lambda I$ reduces the input-side group of (a) and of (b) to $O(n)$. The official and HF CorDA code divide each calibration sample by the absolute value of its largest entry before accumulating $C$, which keeps only positive scalings and coordinate permutations, so (b) describes the CorDA formulas and does not cover shipped CorDA under rotations.

*Proof.* For $r<\min(m,n)$ the translation stabiliser of $\mathcal M_{\le r}$ is $\{0\}$, so covariance of the images (Def II.14) is equivalent to covariance of the splits.

(a) With $L'=hL$, $[W_0h^{-1}hL]_r(hL)^{-1}=[W_0L]_rL^{-1}h^{-1}$, and the result does not depend on the square root chosen, since $[W_0LO]_rO^\top=[W_0L]_r$ for $O\in O(n)$. Also $[W_0L]_r=\Pi_rW_0L$, because the top left singular vectors of $W_0L$ are the top eigenvectors of $W_0LL^\top W_0^\top$. On the output side, the eigenprojector of $gW_0CW_0^\top g^\top$ is $g\Pi_rg^{-1}$ for all $W_0$ exactly when $g$ is conformal (transpose the lemma below).

(b) Lemma: for $1\le s<\min(m,n)$, $[Mh]_s=[M]_sh$ for all $M$ with a gap at $s$ iff $h\in CO(n)$. "If": $M\,cQ$ has the singular vectors of $M$ rotated by $Q$ and the singular values scaled by $c$. "Only if": take $M=\sum_{i\le s+1}\sigma_iu_iv_i^\top$ with distinct $\sigma$'s. The top-$s$ left singular space of $Mh$ is $\mathrm{span}\{u_1,\dots,u_s\}$ only if the coupling $\sigma_s\sigma_{s+1}v_s^\top hh^\top v_{s+1}$ vanishes; requiring this for every orthonormal pair forces $hh^\top\in\mathbb R_{>0}I$. The bottom-$r$ part of $M$ is $M-[M]_{k-r}$, so bottom-$r$ splits follow from the lemma at $s=k-r$. CorDA's covariance requires $[W_0Ch^\top]_sh^{-\top}C^{-1}h^{-1}=[W_0C]_sC^{-1}h^{-1}$, which is the lemma for $h^\top$ applied to $M=W_0C$, an arbitrary matrix. Trace-relative damping commutes with $CO(n)$, since $\mathrm{tr}(c^2QCQ^\top)=c^2\,\mathrm{tr}\,C$. PiSSA and MiLoRA are the lemma for $h^{-1}$. (c) An absolute $\lambda I$ is fixed by $O(n)$ but not rescaled by $c$; the per-sample max-normalisation commutes only with positive scalings and permutations. ∎ [Num: under $h=3Q$ with $Q$ orthogonal, CorDA (IPM and bottom-$r$ KPM), PiSSA and bottom-$r$ MiLoRA move covariantly to $4\times10^{-15}$; under a generic $h$ only the whitened split does ($2\times10^{-15}$, against $0.63$ and $1.1$); at $r=n$ PiSSA is $GL_n$-covariant; damping $0.1$ breaks it under a generic $h$ ($0.14$); under $h=3Q$ an absolute damping $0.1$ breaks the whitened split (about $2\times10^{-2}$), while trace-relative damping keeps it ($3\times10^{-15}$).]

*What this explains.* A design rule from the Erlangen programme. A sufficient condition for a data-aware Type II split to be independent of how a layer's input coordinates are chosen is that it be a left multiple of $W_0$ by a matrix-valued function of the output second moment, $X=\Phi(W_0CW_0^\top)\,W_0$. The whitened (SVD-LLM-style) split and Astra's eigenprojector split are instances; Astra's paper form, a tail eigenprojector of the output covariance, has the same form with $C$ replaced by the centred input covariance, which transforms in the same way. Depending on the data only through $W_0CW_0^\top$ is not enough: PiSSA satisfies that vacuously and is not $GL_n$-covariant. Non-conformal reparametrisations, such as the diagonal rescalings from norm-gain folding or SmoothQuant, change the CorDA and PiSSA splits. Three caveats apply. An absolute damping $C+\lambda I$ reduces the covariance to $O(n)$, while CorDA's trace-relative damping keeps $CO(n)$. The covariance is one-sided. No rank-$r$ split that depends only on $(W_0,C)$ can be $GL_m\times GL_n$-covariant, since the stabiliser of a normal form contains an orthogonal group that fixes only full-rank or zero splits, and whitening with the output second moment itself is degenerate (every singular value of $(W_0CW_0^\top)^{-1/2}W_0L$ is $1$). Descent through hidden-layer $GL$ gauges would need an output-side statistic independent of $(W_0,C)$, such as a backpropagated-gradient second moment. Training dynamics (SGD, Adam) remain non-covariant. EVA is not a split: it is Type I ($B_0=0$, $W_0$ not split). At a fixed rank its image is $GL_m\times GL_n$-covariant under Def II.14, and only its pointing $\mathrm{row}\,A_0$ (the top-$r$ centred principal subspace of the inputs) is $CO(n)$-covariant. With HF's default `rho=2.0` the per-layer rank is redistributed by explained-variance ratios, which are only $CO(n)$-invariant, so the image becomes $GL_m\times CO(n)$-covariant. **[Conj C]** Whitened CorDA or PiSSA is more robust to function-preserving input reparametrisations, and no worse otherwise.

---

## Exposé III. Fibres and metrics: dynamics

Expressivity lives on the image. Training lives on the fibres, through a metric. Two objects of $\mathsf{Meth}$ can be isomorphic and still train differently.

**Definition III.1 (trained method; cometric; three equivalences).** [Def] A *trained method* is a method together with optimizer data: a learning-rate operator $\Lambda\succ0$ (per-block rates), an initial scale, and an update rule.

Gradient flow $\dot q=-\Lambda\nabla_q(L\circ\rho)$ moves the weights by
$$\dot\theta=-K_q\nabla_\theta L,\qquad K_q=D\rho_q\,\Lambda\,D\rho_q^\top.$$
We call $K_q$ the **induced cometric**. For a backward method with compression $c_t$ and anchor $a_t$ (Def IV.1), $K_t=a_tc_t$.

Two methods are:
- **expressively equivalent** if their images agree;
- **first-order equivalent** if their $K_{q_0}$ agree;
- **dynamically equivalent** if they produce the same $\theta$-trajectory for every loss.

**Observation III.2 (chain rule for cometrics).** [Obs] Let $\sigma:Q'\to Q$ and $\rho:Q\to X$ be smooth, with $X=\Theta$ or a function or output space. Train $Q'$ by gradient flow with a constant rate operator $\Lambda'\succ0$, and put $K^\sigma_{q'}:=D\sigma_{q'}\Lambda'D\sigma_{q'}^\top$. Then
$$K^{\rho\sigma}_{q'}=D\rho_{\sigma(q')}\,K^\sigma_{q'}\,D\rho_{\sigma(q')}^\top,$$
and no metric on $Q$ is used. $K$ is a degenerate cometric (PSD, of rank $\le\dim Q'$); when $\sigma$ is not injective, $K^\sigma$ is a field along $\sigma$, not a cometric on $Q$. For LoRA, $\rho(B,A)=W_0+sBA$ with $s=\alpha/r$ ($\alpha/\sqrt r$ for rsLoRA), block rates $\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}(\eta_BI,\eta_AI)$ and no LoRA dropout, under gradient flow (equivalently, to first order in the step size for GD):
$$K_{(B,A)}(G)=s^2\big(\eta_B\,GA^\top A+\eta_A\,BB^\top G\big).$$
Under Adam there is no state-independent linear cometric; Thms III.10 and III.12 treat Adam.

*Proof.* $D(\rho\sigma)=D\rho\,D\sigma$, so $K^{\rho\sigma}=D\rho\,D\sigma\Lambda'D\sigma^\top D\rho^\top$. For LoRA, use $\nabla_B=sGA^\top$, $\nabla_A=sB^\top G$ and $\dot W=s(\dot BA+B\dot A)$. Discrete GD adds the term $s\,\delta B\,\delta A$, of order $\eta^2$. ∎ [Num: finite-difference Jacobians agree to $6\times10^{-9}$.]

*What this explains.* HF `PrefixTuning` with `prefix_projection=True` (Li–Liang's MLP $\sigma$) and with `prefix_projection=False` (the P-tuning v2 setting, ignoring its classification head) have the same prefix image whenever `encoder_hidden_size` $\ge$ `num_virtual_tokens` $-1$. Under gradient flow their cometrics on prefix space are $D\sigma\Lambda'D\sigma^\top$ and $\Lambda$: the reparametrisation replaces the flat metric on prefix space by a point-dependent one. Prefix methods are extensions, so the target here is the prefix (or function) space, not $\Theta$. This alone does not account for the instability Li–Liang observed under AdamW.

**Theorem III.3 (gradient descent is natural exactly where cometrics agree; for LoRA's linear gauge, exactly along $O(r)$).** [Thm]

(a) Let $\varphi:Q\to Q'$ be a diffeomorphism with $\rho'\circ\varphi=\rho$, and let $\Lambda,\Lambda'$ be the rate operators on $Q,Q'$. Under gradient flow, the two methods induce the same $\theta$-velocity at $q$ and at $\varphi(q)$ for every loss iff $K^\rho_q=K^{\rho'}_{\varphi(q)}$, that is,
$$D\rho'_{\varphi(q)}\big(D\varphi_q\Lambda D\varphi_q^\top-\Lambda'\big)D\rho'^\top_{\varphi(q)}=0.$$
This holds whenever $D\varphi\Lambda D\varphi^\top=\Lambda'$, which is an isometry when $\Lambda=\Lambda'=I$. That condition is necessary when $D\rho'_{\varphi(q)}$ is injective, but not in general. Any linear gauge of a linear parametrisation is natural: for $\rho(x,y)=\theta_0+x+y$, the shear $(x,y)\mapsto(2x,y-x)$ preserves $\rho$ and $K$ without being orthogonal. So are pointwise-orthogonal non-linear LoRA gauges $\varphi(q)=\varphi_{g(q)}(q)$ with $g(q)\in O(r)$.

(b) For LoRA's linear gauge $\varphi_g(B,A)=(Bg^{-1},gA)$ with block-scalar rates, at points where $A$ has full row rank ($B$ arbitrary, including $B=0$) and $r<n$, the criterion holds iff $g\in O(r)$. The same holds symmetrically where $B$ has full column rank and $r<m$.

*Proof.* (a) Put $\ell=L\circ\rho'$, so that $L\circ\rho=\ell\circ\varphi$. The $\rho$-flow is $\dot q=-\Lambda D\varphi^\top\nabla\ell$, so $\frac{d}{dt}\varphi(q)=-D\varphi\Lambda D\varphi^\top\nabla\ell$. Apply $D\rho'$ and use $\nabla\ell=D\rho'^\top\nabla_\theta L$. The $\rho'$-flow gives $-D\rho'\Lambda'D\rho'^\top\nabla_\theta L$. Since $\nabla_\theta L$ is arbitrary, the criterion follows. For the examples: a linear $\rho$ has constant $D\rho$ and $D\rho\,P=D\rho$ for a linear gauge $P$. For a pointwise gauge, $D\varphi_q=D\varphi_{g(q)}+V$ with $V$ valued in fibre directions, which $D\rho'$ kills, so the criterion reduces to that of $\varphi_{g(q)}$, covered by (b).

(b) The two cometrics agree for all $G$ iff
$$\eta_B\,GA^\top(g^\top g-I)A=\eta_A\,B(I-g^{-1}g^{-\top})B^\top G\quad\text{for every }G.$$
An identity $GX=YG$ for all $G$ forces $X=cI_n$ and $Y=cI_m$ (test it on the matrix units $E_{ij}$). Here $\operatorname{rank}X\le r<n$, so $c=0$, and full row rank of $A$ gives $g^\top g=I$. Only the full row rank of $A$ is used. ∎ [Num: at a random point a non-orthogonal $g$ gives a cometric mismatch of about $18$, an orthogonal one about $10^{-15}$.]

*What this explains.* No learner with a fixed Euclidean metric respects all invertible reparametrisations. For LoRA's linear gauge, plain gradient descent defines a family of training dynamics indexed by $O(r)\backslash GL_r\cong\mathrm{Sym}^+_r$ (via $g\mapsto g^\top g$). Under gradient descent, LoRA+'s rate ratio $\lambda$ corresponds to the scalar gauge $g=\lambda^{1/4}I$ together with an overall change of rate. rsLoRA is not a gauge choice: it rescales $s$, equivalently the learning rates and the initialisation (Thm III.10, Cor III.11). Riemannian preconditioning and LoRA-Pro remove the dependence (Prop III.4). Under AdamW, as used in practice, the relevant group is $B_r$ (Thm III.12).

**Proposition III.4 (cometrics that descend to the rank-$r$ manifold).** [Prop; the algorithms are Known: Tong–Ma–Chi 2021 and Zhang–Pilanci 2024 (scaled GD); Wang et al. 2024 (LoRA-Pro); Zhang et al. 2023 (LoRA-FA, frozen-$A$ form), with the corrected gradient of (c) from arXiv 2308.03303 v2/v3 (2026) and HF PEFT's LoRA-FA optimizer (2025). The $GL_r$-invariance of the scaled-GD metric and its descent to the quotient $\mathcal M_r$ are classical: Mishra et al. 2012; Mishra–Sepulchre 2016] Write $U=\mathrm{col}\,B$ and $V=\mathrm{row}\,A$. Statements (a), (b) and the first sentence of (d) are on the full-rank locus; (c) needs only $\operatorname{rank}A_0=r$, with $B$ arbitrary, including $B_0=0$. All statements concern gradient flow, or (S)GD to first order, on the stated gradients, undamped and without Adam. Cometrics include the learning rate.

(a) Scaled GD, $\delta B=-\eta\nabla_B(AA^\top)^{-1}$ and $\delta A=-\eta(B^\top B)^{-1}\nabla_A$, is $GL_r$-equivariant with first-order cometric $K(G)=\eta s^2(P_UG+GP_V)$.

(b) LoRA-Pro's equivalent gradient $\tilde g=P_UG+(I-P_U)GP_V$ is the Frobenius-orthogonal projection $\Pi_{T_{W-W_0}\mathcal M_r}(G)$. The flow of its SGD variant (LoRA-Pro Alg. 1) is the Riemannian gradient flow on $W_0+\mathcal M_r$.

(c) LoRA-FA's corrected gradient $s^{-2}\nabla_B(AA^\top)^{-1}$, fed to SGD, gives $K(G)=\eta\,GP_V$, which depends only on $\mathrm{row}A_0\in\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)$. Its original form, plain SGD on $B$, has $K(G)=s^2\eta_BGA_0^\top A_0$.

(d) The cometrics of (a) and (b) are functions of $W$ on the full-rank locus and define vector fields tangent to $W_0+\mathcal M_r$. The field of (c) is $G\mapsto\eta GP_V$ with $V=\mathrm{row}\,A_0$. Its flow is the Frobenius gradient flow on the slice $W_0+\{XA_0\}=W_0+\mathbb R^{m\times n}P_V$, of dimension $mr$, which contains $W_0$ and lies in $W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}$. The rank-$r$ part of the slice, $N_V=\{\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)=V\}$, is open and dense in it and is a submanifold of $W_0+\mathcal M_r$. On $N_V$ the field is tangent to $\mathcal M_r$ and equals $\eta GP_{\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)}$, a function of $W$. So (c) descends too, as a degenerate cometric of rank $mr$ (less than $r(m+n-r)$ when $r<n$) whose flow keeps $\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)=\mathrm{row}\,A_0$ fixed. The cometric of plain GD depends on the representative (it is invariant only under $O(r)$).

*Practice.* Each published method departs from these idealisations, and the departures change the symmetry (Thm III.12):
- the standard start $B_0=0$ lies off the full-rank locus, where (a) and (b) are undefined without damping. Zhang–Pilanci use $(B^\top B+\delta I)^{-1}$ with an unspecified $\delta>0$, which is only $O(r)$-equivariant. Their LLM experiments report both damped scaled GD and scaled AdamW, in which the preconditioner is applied before the Adam moments ($B_r$);
- LoRA-Pro's experiments use its AdamW variant (Alg. 2, with $m\times n$ moments), $\dot W=-\Pi_T(\mathrm{Adam}(\Pi_TG))$. It descends to $W_0+\mathcal M_r$ but is not a gradient flow, and its discrete update with the Sylvester-optimal free matrix is $GL_r$-invariant only to first order. The released code also damps both inverses ($\mathrm{pinv}(\cdot+10^{-8}I)$, so $O(r)$), replaces the step at $B_0=0$ by a LoRA-FA-type step, and runs with HF's default global-norm clipping;
- LoRA-FA as published (v3, Alg. 1) and HF's `create_lorafa_optimizer` apply AdamW after the correction (HF also damps with $10^{-8}I$). The trajectory is then invariant only under signed row permutations of $A_0$ (Thm III.12(b)), so it depends on the basis and scale of $A_0$, not only on $\mathrm{row}A_0$.

*Proof.* (a) Substitute $\nabla_B=sGA^\top$ and $\nabla_A=sB^\top G$ into $\delta W=s(\delta BA+B\delta A)+O(\eta^2)$, using $A^\top(AA^\top)^{-1}A=P_V$ and $B(B^\top B)^{-1}B^\top=P_U$. For equivariance: under $(Bg^{-1},gA)$ we have $\nabla_{B'}=\nabla_Bg^\top$ and $(A'A'^\top)^{-1}=g^{-\top}(AA^\top)^{-1}g^{-1}$, so $\delta B'=\delta Bg^{-1}$; the same holds for $A$.

(b) $T_{W-W_0}\mathcal M_r=\{XA+BY\}=\{D:P_{U^\perp}DP_{V^\perp}=0\}$. The orthogonal projection onto it is $G-P_{U^\perp}GP_{V^\perp}=P_UG+GP_V-P_UGP_V=\tilde g$. For any free matrix $X$ in Alg. 1, $s(h_BA+Bh_A)=P_UG+P_{U^\perp}GP_V$, so $\delta W=-\eta\,\Pi_TG+O(\eta^2)$.

(c) $\Delta W=s\,\Delta B\,A=-\eta\,GA^\top(AA^\top)^{-1}A=-\eta\,GP_V$.

(d) Read off the formulas. On $N_V$, $\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)=V$, so $GP_V=GP_{\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)}$ lies in $\{XA_0\}=T N_V\subseteq T\mathcal M_r$. Rank $r$ is an open condition on the slice, so $N_V$ is open in it. It is dense: $XA_0$ has the rank of $X$, and every $X\in\mathbb R^{m\times r}$ ($m\ge r$) is a limit of rank-$r$ matrices $X+tE$ as $t\to0$, because with $E$ having $I_r$ in its first $r$ rows the top $r\times r$ minor of $X+tE$ is a monic polynomial of degree $r$ in $t$, which vanishes for only finitely many $t$. The operator $G\mapsto\eta GP_V$ has rank $mr$, which is below $r(m+n-r)$ exactly when $r<n$. ∎ [Num: gauge defects $\sim10^{-15}$ for (a), against $52$ for plain GD; LoRA-FA with AdamW from $A_0$ and from $gA_0$ differs by $O(1)$ after a few steps, against $10^{-15}$ with SGD.]

*What this explains.* Under (S)GD each method is summarised by its induced weight-space cometric. LoRA, scaled GD and LoRA-Pro share the image $W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}$ and differ only in cometric. LoRA-FA is the sub-object $W_0+\{XA_0\}$ (Cor III.13). GaLore (with a gradient-SVD or a random projector) and MeZO are full-parameter methods, listed for comparison.

| method (gradient flow / SGD) | cometric $K(G)$ |
|---|---|
| LoRA | $s^2(\eta_BGA^\top A+\eta_ABB^\top G)$ |
| LoRA-FA (original / corrected) | $s^2\eta_BGA_0^\top A_0$ / $\eta GP_V$ |
| scaled GD (undamped Riemannian preconditioning) | $\eta s^2(P_UG+GP_V)$ |
| LoRA-Pro (SGD variant) | $\eta\,\Pi_{T\mathcal M_r}G$ |
| GaLore (one period) | $\eta\alpha PP^\top G$, or $\eta\alpha GQQ^\top$ when $m>n$ (default $\alpha=0.25$) |
| GaLore with a random projector $A_t$ (one period) | $\eta GA_t^\top A_t$ |
| MeZO | $\eta zz^\top G$, equal to $\eta G$ in expectation |

"Transformation invariance" in the sense of LoRA-RITE's Definition 1 means that the $W$-update is invariant under the gauge ($W$-level descent), which is weaker than parameter-level $GL_r$-equivariance. LoRA-RITE's algorithm satisfies the stronger property on the full-rank locus (Thm III.12).

**Theorem III.5 (Noether charges and isometries for cotangent signals).** [Known: Zhao et al. 2023, Prop. 5.1, including the symmetric/antisymmetric table and symmetric learning rates; Marcotte–Gribonval–Peyré 2023 for laws independent of loss and data; the principle: Du–Hu–Lee 2018, Arora–Cohen–Hazan 2018, Kunin et al. 2021. New here: only the remark that $\gamma$ need not be a gradient, which is formally immediate (along the realised trajectory $\gamma(t)$ is the gradient of the time-dependent linear loss $\langle\gamma(t),\theta\rangle$), and the PEFT instances.] Let a Lie group act linearly on $Q=\mathbb R^N$ with $\rho\circ k=\rho$, let $\Lambda\succ0$ be constant, and let $\gamma(t)\in T^*_{\rho(q(t))}\Theta$ be **any** cotangent signal, not necessarily a gradient. Consider continuous-time updates
$$\dot q=-\Lambda\,D\rho_q^\top\gamma(t).$$
(i) For every generator $\xi$ with $\Lambda^{-1}\xi$ symmetric, the charge $J_\xi(q)=\tfrac12\langle q,\Lambda^{-1}\xi q\rangle$ is conserved.

(ii) For every generator with $\Lambda^{-1}\xi$ antisymmetric, $k=e^{s\xi}$ preserves $\langle\cdot,\Lambda^{-1}\cdot\rangle$, so $K_{kq}=K_q$ along the orbit: for the same cotangent $\gamma$, the $\theta$-velocities at $q$ and at $kq$ agree. If moreover $\gamma$ depends on $q$ only through $(t,\rho(q))$, e.g. $\gamma=\nabla_\theta L$ or an open-loop signal, and solutions are unique ($\gamma$ measurable in $t$ and locally Lipschitz in $\theta$, $\rho$ of class $C^{1,1}_{\rm loc}$), the $\theta$-trajectories from $q_0$ and from $kq_0$ coincide. If $\gamma$ uses Euclidean norms on $Q$, as global-norm clipping does, the trajectory statement holds if, in addition, $k$ is a Euclidean isometry, and can fail otherwise. So (i) and the velocity statement of (ii) hold for arbitrary $\gamma$, while the trajectory statement needs a gradient-like signal.

(iii) When $\mathfrak g$ is closed under $\xi\mapsto\Lambda\xi^\top\Lambda^{-1}$, every generator splits into a part of type (i) and a part of type (ii), a Cartan decomposition $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak k\oplus\mathfrak p$. This holds for LoRA's $\mathfrak{gl}_r$ with per-block scalar rates. Without it, there are generators of neither type. With per-rank-channel rates $\Lambda(B,A)=(BD_B,D_AA)$, $\xi_X$ is of type (i) iff $XD_B^{-1}$ and $D_A^{-1}X$ are both symmetric, and of type (ii) iff both are antisymmetric. An off-diagonal pair $(i,j)$ contributes one charge and one isometry iff $\eta_{B,i}\eta_{A,i}=\eta_{B,j}\eta_{A,j}$, and $\mathfrak g$ is closed iff all the products $\eta_{B,j}\eta_{A,j}$ are equal. In that case one gets a $D$-conjugate of $\mathfrak{so}_r\oplus\mathrm{Sym}_r$ whose isometries are not all Euclidean unless the rates are block-scalar, so the clipping caveat of (ii) applies. For pairwise distinct products, only the diagonal $X$ give charges and no nonzero $X$ gives an isometry.

*Proof.* Invariance of $\rho$ gives $D\rho_q(\xi q)=0$. Hence
$$\tfrac{d}{dt}J_\xi=\langle\Lambda^{-1}\xi q,\dot q\rangle=-\langle\xi q,D\rho_q^\top\gamma\rangle=-\langle D\rho_q\xi q,\gamma\rangle=0.$$
For (ii), $\Lambda^{-1}\xi$ antisymmetric gives $\tfrac{d}{ds}(e^{s\xi^\top}\Lambda^{-1}e^{s\xi})=0$, equivalently $k\Lambda k^\top=\Lambda$. From $\rho\circ k=\rho$ we get $D\rho_q=D\rho_{kq}k$, so $K_q=D\rho_{kq}k\Lambda k^\top D\rho_{kq}^\top=K_{kq}$, and $\tfrac{d}{dt}(kq)=-k\Lambda k^\top D\rho_{kq}^\top\gamma=-\Lambda D\rho_{kq}^\top\gamma$. When $\gamma$ factors through $(t,\rho)$, $kq(t)$ solves the same ODE as the trajectory from $kq_0$, and uniqueness concludes. (iii) Write $\xi=\tfrac12(\xi+\Lambda\xi^\top\Lambda^{-1})+\tfrac12(\xi-\Lambda\xi^\top\Lambda^{-1})$. For per-channel rates, $\langle(B',A'),\Lambda^{-1}\xi_X(B,A)\rangle=\mathrm{tr}(B'^\top BXD_B^{-1})-\mathrm{tr}(A'^\top D_A^{-1}XA)$, which is symmetric (antisymmetric) in the two arguments iff $XD_B^{-1}$ and $D_A^{-1}X$ are. For $X=xE_{ij}+yE_{ji}$ the two conditions read $y=x\,\eta_{B,i}/\eta_{B,j}$ and $y=x\,\eta_{A,j}/\eta_{A,i}$ (with signs flipped in the antisymmetric case), which agree iff $\eta_{B,i}\eta_{A,i}=\eta_{B,j}\eta_{A,j}$. ∎ [Num: with a non-gradient, $q$-dependent $\gamma$ on LoRA, $\Phi$ drifts by $1.6\times10^{-12}$; with Euclidean clipping and a non-Euclidean isometry $k$, the $\theta$-trajectories from $q_0$ and $kq_0$ differ by $0.06$; with per-channel rates $\eta_B=(1,1,1)$, $\eta_A=(1,1,2)$ at $r=3$, closure fails but $E_{12}+E_{21}$ still gives a conserved charge.]

*Instances.* All instances assume gradient flow, or SGD without momentum, with per-block rates. AdamW and heavy-ball momentum fall outside the theorem, since the momentum buffer stores $D\rho$ at past points.
- **LoRA.** The generators $\xi_X(B,A)=(BX,-XA)$ with $\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}(\eta_B,\eta_A)$ satisfy $\Lambda^{-1}\xi_X$ symmetric iff $X=X^\top$. So the $r(r+1)/2$ charges are the entries of
$$\Phi=\frac{B^\top B}{\eta_B}-\frac{AA^\top}{\eta_A},$$
which is "balancedness", the $\mathfrak p=\mathrm{Sym}_r$ half of $\mathfrak{gl}_r=\mathfrak{so}_r\oplus\mathrm{Sym}_r$. The $\mathfrak{so}_r$ generators are Euclidean isometries, so (ii) holds for LoRA even under global-norm clipping.
- **DyLoRA.** The gauge reduces to the torus, so only $\mathrm{diag}\,\Phi$ is conserved.
- **VeRA.** The gauge is $(b,d)\mapsto(cb,d/c)$, with charge $\|b\|^2/\eta_b-\|d\|^2/\eta_d$. NOLA is analogous.
- **AdaLoRA** ($P\Lambda Q$), without its orthogonality regulariser and between rank-budget masking events. Each torus edge gives a charge: $\|p_k\|^2-\lambda_k^2$ and $\|q_k\|^2-\lambda_k^2$, at equal rates. The regularised objective (HF default `orth_reg_weight=0.5`) breaks these charges.
- **HRA.** $u_i\mapsto cu_i$ gives a conserved $\|u_i\|^2$.
- **ReLU bottleneck adapters.** Positive per-unit rescalings give one charge per hidden unit.
- **TT adapters.** One conserved matrix per bond.
- **DoRA's detached-norm backward and global-norm gradient clipping.** Both still have the form $\Lambda D\rho_{\rm LoRA}^\top\gamma$, with $\gamma$ the detached or clipped signal, so **$\Phi$ is still conserved**.

Under discrete GD with rates $\eta_B,\eta_A$, one step changes $\Phi$ by exactly $\eta_BAG^\top GA^\top-\eta_AB^\top GG^\top B$: the drift of $\eta\Phi$, or the drift relative to $\Phi$, is $O(\eta^2)$ per step. Adam is not of this form, so the drift of $\Phi$ under Adam measures how far Adam departs from the gauge geometry **[Pred]**.

**Corollary III.6 (charge at initialisation; weight decay).** [Cor]

(a) $\Phi_0=B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B-A_0A_0^\top/\eta_A$ is the value of the conserved charge, fixed by the initialisation and the rates. Since $O(r)$ acts on $\Phi$ by conjugation and preserves $\theta$-trajectories (Thm III.5(ii)), only its spectrum labels a $\theta$-trajectory.

| initialisation | $\Phi_0$ |
|---|---|
| HF default LoRA on Linear/Conv layers ($B_0=0$; Kaiming-uniform $A_0$, entries $U(-n^{-1/2},n^{-1/2})$, with $n$ the fan-in, $\mathrm{in\_channels}\times$ kernel size for Conv) | $-A_0A_0^\top/\eta_A$, with $\mathbb E[A_0A_0^\top]=\tfrac13I_r$ and fluctuations $O(n^{-1/2})$ on and off the diagonal |
| HF default LoRA on embedding layers ($A_0=0$, normal $B_0$) | $+B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B$ (Init[B]-type) |
| HF `mica` ($A_0=0$, frozen minor-singular $B_0$) | $+B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B$ (Init[B]-type, with $B$ frozen) |
| EVA (orthonormal rows; default `whiten=False`) | $-I/\eta_A$ exactly |
| PiSSA in HF PEFT, and MiLoRA if implemented like `pissa_init` with the bottom-$r$ singular values (MiLoRA is not in HF PEFT) ($B_0^\top B_0=A_0A_0^\top=S_r/s$, with $S_r$ the top-$r$, resp. bottom-$r$, singular values and $s$ the LoRA scale) | $(S_r/s)(1/\eta_B-1/\eta_A)$, zero **iff** $\eta_A=\eta_B$ (when $S_r\ne0$) |
| OLoRA ($B_0=Q_r$, $A_0=R_r$) | $I/\eta_B-R_rR_r^\top/\eta_A$ |
| "Init[B]" ($A_0=0$) | $+B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B$ |

(b) Let $G$ be any cotangent signal, with the scale $s$ absorbed into it. Under gradient flow, if decay acts per unit time and identically on both factors ($\dot B=-\eta_BGA^\top-\lambda B$, $\dot A=-\eta_AB^\top G-\lambda A$), then $\dot\Phi=-2\lambda\Phi$. With learning-rate-coupled decay (PyTorch SGD `weight_decay`; the decay term of PyTorch AdamW), $\dot B=-\eta_B(GA^\top+\lambda B)$ and likewise for $A$. Then $\Phi(t)=e^{-2\eta\lambda t}\Phi(0)$ holds when $\eta_A=\eta_B=\eta$. For $\eta_A\ne\eta_B$ it fails in general: $\dot\Phi=-2\lambda(B^\top B-AA^\top)$, which is not a function of $\Phi$. With an actual Adam(W) gradient term, $\Phi$ drifts even without decay, at first order in the step size; Adam lies outside Thm III.5 [Num]. So neither law describes AdamW training.

*Proof.* (a) Substitute the initialisations. Each Kaiming entry has variance $1/(3n)$ and there are $n$ entries per row, so the diagonal has mean $1/3$ with fluctuations $O(n^{-1/2})$, and the off-diagonal entries have standard deviation $1/(3\sqrt n)$. HF's `pissa_init` divides $S_r$ by the scale before splitting.

(b) Differentiate:
$$\tfrac{d}{dt}\tfrac{B^\top B}{\eta_B}=-(AG^\top B+B^\top GA^\top)-2c_B\tfrac{B^\top B}{\eta_B},$$
with $c_B=\lambda$ in the per-time convention and $c_B=\eta_B\lambda$ in the coupled one. Do the same for $A$ and subtract. ∎ [Num: testing $\Phi(t)=e^{-2\eta_A\lambda t}\Phi(0)$ at $\eta_B/\eta_A=4$ with coupled decay gives relative error $10.7$; $2\times10^{-4}$ (Euler error) in the valid cases; one Adam sign step moves $\Phi$ by an amount proportional to the learning rate.]

*What this explains.*
- Zero-product initialisations keep their charge under gradient flow, and that charge is maximally unbalanced (Prop III.8). The *relative* imbalance $\|\Phi\|/(\|B\|^2/\eta_B+\|A\|^2/\eta_A)$ decays as the adapter grows (Thm III.9).
- HF PiSSA always has $\mu_0=B_0^\top B_0-A_0A_0^\top=0$ (unweighted-balanced), and $\Phi_0=0$ only at equal rates. So it starts on the slice that coupled decay targets, but off the per-time-decay slice when $\eta_A\ne\eta_B$.
- Both decay conventions gauge-fix at equilibrium, because each is a $\Lambda$-gradient flow of $L$ plus a fibre-wise Kempf–Ness potential. Per-time decay is the flow of $L+\tfrac\lambda2(\|B\|^2/\eta_B+\|A\|^2/\eta_A)$; it drives $\Phi\to0$ exponentially, onto the rate-weighted slice $B^\top B/\eta_B=AA^\top/\eta_A$. Coupled decay is the flow of $L+\tfrac\lambda2(\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2)$; its equilibria lie on the unweighted balanced slice $\mu=B^\top B-AA^\top=0$ of Prop III.7(a), with no closed-form law for $\Phi$ unless $\eta_A=\eta_B$. The two slices coincide iff $\eta_A=\eta_B$. This corrects an earlier claim that coupled decay fails to gauge-fix at LoRA+ rates. [Num: at $\eta_B/\eta_A=4$, coupled decay converges to $\mu=0$ to $10^{-12}$ while $\Phi$ stays $O(1)$; per-time decay converges to $\Phi=0$ while $\mu$ stays $O(1)$.]

**Proposition III.7 (Kempf–Ness slice; Loewner floor).** [Prop; Known: Kempf–Ness 1979, in the real form of Richardson–Slodowy 1990; Srebro–Rennie–Jaakkola, NIPS 17 (2004)]

(a) On a full-rank $GL_r$-orbit, the critical points of $\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2$ are exactly the balanced representatives, those with $\mu=B^\top B-AA^\top=0$. They form one $O(r)$-orbit, they are the minima, and the minimum value is $2\|BA\|_*$.

(b) Assume gradient flow with block-scalar rates $\eta_A,\eta_B$, no weight decay, a loss that depends on $(B,A)$ only through $BA$, and any cotangent signal of the form $\Lambda D\rho^\top\gamma$ (dropout, minibatches and clipping included). From $B_0=0$,
$$B^\top B=\tfrac{\eta_B}{\eta_A}\big(AA^\top-A_0A_0^\top\big)\succeq0,$$
so $AA^\top\succeq A_0A_0^\top$ for all time: the $A$-factor never shrinks below its initialisation in the Loewner order. $AA^\top(t)$ need not be monotone in $t$; only this floor is guaranteed. Discrete steps break the bound by $O(\eta^2)$ per step. Under Adam it is not guaranteed and fails empirically. With per-time decay $\lambda$, only $AA^\top\succeq e^{-2\lambda t}A_0A_0^\top$ holds.

*Proof.* (a) For $\xi\in\mathfrak{gl}_r$ with symmetric part $\xi_s$,
$$\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_0\big(\|Be^{-t\xi}\|^2+\|e^{t\xi}A\|^2\big)=-2\operatorname{tr}(\xi_s\mu).$$
This vanishes for all $\xi$ iff $\mu=0$. The function is $O(r)$-invariant, so by the polar decomposition $GL_r=O(r)\exp(\mathrm{Sym}_r)$ it suffices to study $h(t)=f(e^{t\xi}\cdot x)$ for symmetric $\xi=P\,\mathrm{diag}(d)P^\top$. Then
$$h''(t)=4\sum_id_i^2\big(\|(BP)_{:,i}\|^2e^{-2td_i}+\|(P^\top A)_{i,:}\|^2e^{2td_i}\big)\ge0,$$
so $h$ is convex and critical points are minima. If $x$ and $e^{\xi}\cdot x$ are both balanced, then $h'(0)=h'(1)=0$, so $h''\equiv0$ on $[0,1]$. Since $B$ has full column rank, every $(BP)_{:,i}\ne0$, which forces all $d_i=0$. Hence the balanced points form one $O(r)$-orbit. Existence: for $BA=U\Sigma V^\top$, the representative $B=U\Sigma^{1/2}O$, $A=O^\top\Sigma^{1/2}V^\top$ is balanced, with value $2\operatorname{tr}\Sigma$.

(b) Conservation of $\Phi$ (Thm III.5) with $B_0=0$; for per-time decay, Cor III.6(b). ∎ [Num: on 5 random orbits at $(m,n,r)=(7,5,3)$ the minimum equals $2\|BA\|_*$ to $10^{-14}$.]

*What this explains.* "Balanced" is not a heuristic: it is the Kempf–Ness slice of the gauge action. An explicit penalty $\lambda(\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2)$ is equivalent at its minimisers to a $(2\lambda/s)$-nuclear-norm penalty on $\Delta W=sBA$ restricted to rank $\le r$; SGD-coupled decay with coefficient $\lambda$ is the flow of $L+\tfrac\lambda2(\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2)$ and matches a $(\lambda/s)$-nuclear-norm penalty (Srebro et al., whose argument also covers rank-deficient factorisations). In both cases the stationary points lie on $\mu=0$. With per-time decay at unequal rates the relevant slice is $\Phi=0$ (Cor III.6). Decoupled AdamW decay is not covered.

**Proposition III.8 (the balance–escape trilemma).** [Prop] No LoRA initialisation is simultaneously
- (a) identity-at-init without a residual split ($B_0A_0=0$),
- (b) balanced ($B_0^\top B_0=cA_0A_0^\top$ for some $c>0$: unweighted, $c=1$, or rate-weighted, $\Phi_0=0$ with $c=\eta_B/\eta_A$), and
- (c) non-stationary: $D\rho$ at $(B_0,A_0)$ is not the zero map; equivalently, $\dot W(0)\ne0$ for some parameter velocity, under any first-order optimizer.

Moreover, if $B_0A_0=0$ then $\langle B_0^\top B_0,A_0A_0^\top\rangle_F=0$, so $\|B_0^\top B_0-cA_0A_0^\top\|_F^2=\|B_0^\top B_0\|_F^2+c^2\|A_0A_0^\top\|_F^2$: zero-product initialisations are maximally unbalanced.

*Proof.* Assume (a) and (b). Then $0=B_0^\top B_0A_0=cA_0A_0^\top A_0$, so $(A_0^\top A_0)^2=0$. Since $A_0^\top A_0$ is PSD, it vanishes, so $A_0=0$ and then $B_0=0$. At $(0,0)$, $D\rho(\dot B,\dot A)=s(\dot BA+B\dot A)=0$, and also $\nabla_B=\nabla_A=0$; so every gradient-based update rule, including Adam, momentum and weight decay, is stationary there. For the identity, $\operatorname{tr}(B_0^\top B_0A_0A_0^\top)=\operatorname{tr}(B_0^\top(B_0A_0)A_0^\top)=0$. ∎

*What this explains.* The field splits into two families of initialisation.
- **Type I** keeps $\rho$ and picks $q_0$ in the apex fibre $\{BA=0\}$: LoRA, EVA, Init[B]. By the identity above these are maximally unbalanced.
- **Type II** changes $\rho$ by an exact split $\theta_0=\theta_{\rm res}+\delta(q_0)$: PiSSA, MiLoRA, OLoRA, LoRA-GA, CorDA. LoftQ and QPiSSA are approximate splits over a quantised residual and are not identity-at-init (Prop V.2).

The proposition forces every balanced, non-stationary, identity-at-init LoRA initialisation to be Type II. The converse fails: OLoRA ($B_0^\top B_0=I$, $A_0A_0^\top=R_rR_r^\top$) and CorDA are Type II and generally unbalanced. A frozen residual is the price of any nonzero $B_0A_0$, and so in particular of balance, as in PiSSA and MiLoRA at equal rates. By Thm II.2, Type II objects are different objects, incomparable with LoRA.

**Theorem III.9 (closed-form isotropic-charge LoRA dynamics).** [Known: Tarmoun–França–Haeffele–Vidal (ICML 2021), who call this charge "$\lambda$-balanced"; Min–Tarmoun–Vidal–Mallada (ICML 2021); see also Dominé et al. 2024 and Kunin et al. 2024. The PEFT reading is ours.] Let $\rho(B,A)=W_{\rm res}+sBA$ be trained by gradient flow with rates $\eta_A,\eta_B$, and suppose the charge is isotropic, $\Phi=-\kappa I_r$. Put $\mu=s\sqrt{\eta_A\eta_B}$, and on the locus where $Z=W-W_{\rm res}$ has rank $r$ write its compact SVD $Z=U\Sigma V^\top$. Then
$$\dot W=-\mu^2\Big[G\,V g_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)V^\top+U f_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)U^\top G\Big],\qquad f_\kappa(x)=\sqrt{x+\tfrac{\kappa^2}4}-\tfrac\kappa2,\quad g_\kappa=f_\kappa+\kappa .$$
The identity holds pointwise on $\{\Phi=-\kappa I,\ \operatorname{rank}Z=r\}$; gradient flow is used only to keep $\Phi$ fixed. The two terms under the root are equal at the **crossover scale** $\sigma^*=\mu\lvert\kappa\rvert/2$.
- For $\sigma\ll\sigma^*$ with $\kappa>0$, $f\to0$ and $g\to\kappa$, so $\dot W\approx-\mu^2\kappa\,GP_V$. These are LoRA-FA dynamics: $A$'s contribution to $\dot W$ is $O(\sigma^2/(\mu^2\kappa))$, although $A$ itself still drifts slowly. For $\kappa<0$ the roles swap, $\dot W\approx-\mu^2\lvert\kappa\rvert P_UG$, and only $A$ effectively moves (Init[B]).
- For $\sigma\gg\sigma^*$, $f\approx g\approx\sigma/\mu$, so $\dot W\approx-\mu\big[G(Z^\top Z)^{1/2}+(ZZ^\top)^{1/2}G\big]$. These are the balanced dynamics of Arora–Cohen–Hazan.
- *Off the rank-$r$ locus.* For $\kappa\ge0$, $T=f_\kappa(ZZ^\top/\mu^2)$ still holds as a full matrix function, but $S$ gains the term $\kappa(P_{\mathrm{row}A}-P_V)$; symmetrically, for $\kappa<0$, $T$ gains $\lvert\kappa\rvert(P_{\mathrm{col}B}-P_U)$. At the default-LoRA start $Z=0$ ($B_0=0$) this gives $\dot W=-\mu^2\kappa\,GP_{\mathrm{row}A_0}$, so the $\sigma\ll\sigma^*$ regime extends continuously to $t=0$.

*Proof.* Put $\beta=B/\sqrt{\eta_B}$ and $\alpha=A/\sqrt{\eta_A}$. Then $\beta^\top\beta-\alpha\alpha^\top=-\kappa I$ and $\beta\alpha=Z/\mu$. Let $T=\beta\beta^\top$ and $S=\alpha^\top\alpha$.
- $ZZ^\top/\mu^2=\beta(\beta^\top\beta+\kappa I)\beta^\top=T^2+\kappa T$.
- $Z^\top Z/\mu^2=S^2-\kappa S$.
- On the rank-$r$ locus, $\alpha\alpha^\top\succ0$ and $\beta^\top\beta\succ0$. The nonzero eigenvalues of $T$ equal those of $\beta^\top\beta=\alpha\alpha^\top-\kappa I$, so they exceed $\max(0,-\kappa)$; those of $S$ exceed $\max(0,\kappa)$.
- On these ranges, $t\mapsto t^2+\kappa t$ and $s\mapsto s^2-\kappa s$ are strictly increasing and send $0$ to $0$, with positive roots $f_\kappa$ and $g_\kappa$. The compact SVD matters here: $f_\kappa(0)=\lvert\kappa\rvert$ for $\kappa<0$ and $g_\kappa(0)=\kappa$ for $\kappa>0$, so the full matrix function would be wrong on $\ker Z$. Hence $T=Uf_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)U^\top$ and $S=Vg_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)V^\top$.
- Finally, $\dot W=-s^2(\eta_BGA^\top A+\eta_ABB^\top G)=-\mu^2(GS+TG)$.

Off the locus, for $\kappa>0$, $S=\alpha^\top\alpha$ also has the eigenvalue $\kappa$ on $\mathrm{row}A\ominus\mathrm{row}Z$, and symmetrically for $\kappa<0$. ∎ [Num: the formula matches direct integration to $10^{-12}$, and the pointwise identity holds to $10^{-15}$ for $\kappa\in\{-2,-0.3,0,0.3,2,10\}$ and all shape cases.]

*What this explains.* Call this the PEFT reading; it is scoped to gradient flow and SGD.
- **Default LoRA.** With $B_0=0$ and $A_0A_0^\top=cI$, $\kappa=c/\eta_A$ and $\sigma^*=\tfrac{sc}2\sqrt{\eta_B/\eta_A}$; HF's default gives $c\approx1/3$.
- **LoRA+** ($\lambda=\eta_B/\eta_A$) multiplies $\sigma^*$ by $\sqrt\lambda$, and its balanced-regime rate is the geometric mean $s\sqrt{\eta_A\eta_B}$. LoRA-FA is the endpoint $\lambda\to\infty$ of the LoRA+ family.
- **PiSSA at equal rates** has $\kappa=0$ and is exactly balanced forever under gradient flow; SGD drifts by $O(\eta^2)$ per step, and Adam does not preserve it.
- **Exactness.** The theorem is exact for EVA- and LoRA-GA-type orthonormal frames. For Kaiming initialisations it holds only approximately: the eigenvalues of $A_0A_0^\top$ lie near $c(1\pm\sqrt{r/n})^2$ (Marchenko–Pastur), so the crossover is a band of relative width $O(\sqrt{r/n})$ rather than a single scale. At $r=256$, $n=4096$, the eigenvalues of $3A_0A_0^\top$ span roughly $0.57$ to $1.57$.
- **[Pred]** Under SGD, singular values of $\Delta W$ switch from one-sided to two-sided growth as they cross $\sigma^*$.

**Theorem III.10 (the hyperparameter torus).** [Thm; the LoRA/Adam case, including the $\varepsilon>0$ extension and the two effective hyperparameters, is Known: Schulman et al. 2025, "LoRA Without Regret". New here: arbitrary multihomogeneous $\delta$ and the SGD/heavy-ball $c^2$ law] Let $\rho(q)=\theta_b+s\,\delta(q_1,\dots,q_k)$ with $\delta$ multihomogeneous of multidegree $(d_1,\dots,d_k)$. Train block $i$ from $q_i(0)=\sigma_iu_i$ with Adam ($\beta_1\in[0,1)$, $\beta_2\in(0,1)$, bias correction allowed) at learning rate $\eta_i(t)$, on the loss $L\circ\rho$ with no $q$-dependent regulariser. Assume no gradient clipping (or per-block clipping with thresholds rescaled as below), common random numbers (the same data order and dropout masks in both runs), and exact arithmetic (the result is bit-exact in floating point when every $c_i$ is a power of $2$). Then the trajectory $t\mapsto\rho(q(t))$ is invariant under the torus action of $c\in\mathbb R_{>0}^k$:
$$\big(s,\ \eta_i(t),\ \sigma_i,\ \varepsilon_i,\ \lambda_i,\ C_i\big)\mapsto\Big(s\prod_ic_i^{-d_i},\ c_i\eta_i(t),\ c_i\sigma_i,\ \varepsilon_i/c_i,\ \lambda_i/c_i,\ C_i/c_i\Big).$$
Here $\varepsilon_i$ is a per-block Adam $\varepsilon$ (with the convention $0/0=0$ at $\varepsilon=0$), $\lambda_i$ a per-block decoupled (AdamW) weight decay, and $C_i$ a per-block clipping threshold. Under SGD, with or without heavy-ball or Nesterov momentum, the same holds with $\eta_i(t)\mapsto c_i^2\eta_i(t)$ and coupled $L^2$ decay $\lambda_i\mapsto\lambda_i/c_i^2$. In particular, any learning-rate schedule shared by all blocks, warm-up included, is preserved.

*Proof.* The rescaling $q_i'=c_iq_i$, together with the compensating $s$, is an isomorphism in $\mathsf{Meth}$. At corresponding points, $\nabla_{q_i'}=c_i^{-1}\nabla_{q_i}$. With $\varepsilon_i'=\varepsilon_i/c_i$, Adam's normalised step is invariant to the positive rescaling $c_i^{-1}$ of a block's whole gradient history, so $\Delta q_i'=c_i\Delta q_i$ requires $\eta_i'(t)=c_i\eta_i(t)$, and the decoupled decay term $\eta_i'\lambda_i'q_i'=c_i\eta_i\lambda_iq_i$ requires $\lambda_i'=\lambda_i/c_i$. For SGD and momentum the buffers are linear in the gradients, so the gradient factor $c_i^{-1}$ combines with the required $c_i$ to give $c_i^2$, and coupled decay enters the gradient as $\lambda_i'q_i'$, giving $\lambda_i/c_i^2$. Clipping decisions agree when the thresholds scale like the gradients. The time-dependence of $\eta_i$ plays no role. Conclude by induction over steps. Floating point: if every $c_i$ is a power of $2$, multiplication by $c_i^{\pm1}$ and by $\prod_ic_i^{\mp d_i}$ is exact and commutes with every correctly rounded addition, multiplication, division and square root, barring overflow and underflow (exact power-of-two scaling in IEEE arithmetic; Higham 2002, Ch. 2). Then the forward pass gives bit-identical weights, the backward pass gives gradients exactly $c_i^{-1}$ times the original ones, and every step of the induction holds exactly, so the two runs agree bit for bit. ∎ [Num: trajectory differences are exactly $0.0$ for power-of-two $c$ and about $10^{-15}$ otherwise, including a warm-up schedule and rescaled $\varepsilon$ and decay; with $\varepsilon$ or decay held fixed, or with global-norm clipping, they are $10^{-3}$ to $O(1)$.]

**Corollary III.11 (LoRA+ is $\alpha$ in disguise; rsLoRA is LoRA).** [Cor; (a) and the LoRA+ reading are Known: Schulman et al. 2025] For linear LoRA modules ($k=2$, $d=(1,1)$, $\sigma_B=0$), under the hypotheses of Thm III.10:

(a) **Effective hyperparameters.** Under Adam the torus invariants of $(s,\eta_A,\eta_B,\sigma_A)$ are $(s\eta_A\eta_B,\ \sigma_A/\eta_A)$; Schulman et al.'s $\alpha\cdot\mathrm{init}_A\cdot\mathrm{LR}_B$ and $\mathrm{init}_A/\mathrm{LR}_A$ are equivalent generators. Under SGD the invariants are $(s^2\eta_A\eta_B,\ \sigma_A^2/\eta_A)$.

(b) **LoRA+.** Under Adam with $\varepsilon=0$, no weight decay, no gradient clipping and a learning-rate schedule shared by both groups, LoRA+ with ratio $\lambda$ at scale $s$ produces *exactly* the trajectory of plain LoRA at scale $\lambda s$ (same $\eta_A$, same $A_0$). For $\varepsilon>0$ the equivalence needs a per-block $\varepsilon_B'=\lambda\varepsilon$, and with AdamW a per-group decay $\lambda\cdot\mathrm{wd}$ on $B$. Under SGD it holds at scale $\sqrt\lambda\,s$, or equivalently with plain LoRA at the single rate $\sqrt{\eta_A\eta_B}$ and $A_0\mapsto\lambda^{1/4}A_0$.

(c) **rsLoRA.** $\rho_{\rm rs}(B,A)=\rho_{\rm LoRA}(\sqrt r\,B,A)$ is an isomorphism of objects. Up to the hyperparameter torus, rsLoRA can be represented as LoRA at the same $\alpha$ with $\eta_B$ multiplied by $\sqrt r$ under Adam (by $r$ under SGD), or equally as LoRA with $\alpha$ multiplied by $\sqrt r$ at the same rates. The reading "same object, different metric" is a choice of representative within the orbit. (c) concerns a fixed rank; rsLoRA's rank-stability prescription, how $\alpha$ should scale with $r$, is not addressed.

*Proof.* (a) These are the invariants of the 2-torus action. (b) Take $c_B=1/\lambda$, $c_A=1$ (Adam) or $c_B=\lambda^{-1/2}$ (SGD); the single-rate form is $c_A=\lambda^{1/4}$, $c_B=\lambda^{-1/4}$. Thm III.10 gives $\varepsilon_B'=\varepsilon/c_B$ and $\mathrm{wd}_B'=\mathrm{wd}/c_B$. (c) Take $c_B=\sqrt r$. ∎ [Num: the maximum trajectory difference is exactly $0.0$ at $\varepsilon=0$ for power-of-two $\lambda$ and about $10^{-14}$ otherwise, against $0.93$ for the unrescaled control; $5\times10^{-9}$ at a shared $\varepsilon=10^{-8}$; $0.6$ with global-norm clipping at $0.5$.]

*What this explains.* LoRA's four knobs are coordinates on a 4-dimensional space acted on by a 2-torus, and only the orbit matters. LoRA+, rsLoRA and $\alpha$-tuning therefore collapse into one family. The slogan depends on the chart: holding $\eta_A$ fixed, LoRA+ is "$\alpha$ multiplied by $\lambda$"; holding $\eta_B$ fixed, it is plain LoRA with $\eta_A$ divided by $\lambda$, a slower $A$-timescale. This corrects the earlier claim that Adam makes the LoRA+ ratio non-absorbable. **[Pred]** Any gain of LoRA+ over LoRA with $\alpha$ re-tuned by the factor $\lambda$ must come from the ingredients that break the extended torus: global-norm clipping (on by default in the HF Trainer, `max_grad_norm=1.0`), $\varepsilon$ or weight decay held equal across parameter groups (as in HF PEFT's LoRA+ optimizer), and learning-rate schedules that differ between groups. Shared schedules and warm-up preserve the torus. HF's LoRA+ optimizer treats embedding adapters and 1-D parameters separately, so the statement covers linear modules only.

**Theorem III.12 (the optimizer gauge hierarchy).** [Thm] On LoRA's full-rank locus, with hyperparameters shared by all rank channels, the largest subgroup of the gauge $GL_r$ under which an update rule is equivariant, at the level of parameters, is as follows.

(a) SGD and GD with any block rates, with or without heavy-ball momentum: $O(r)$.

(b) Adam and AdamW at fixed learning rates ($\beta_1,\beta_2\in[0,1)$, any $\varepsilon\ge0$, any weight decay): the signed permutations $B_r$, of order $2^rr!$. A positive diagonal $g=\mathrm{diag}(d)$ is a symmetry only *jointly* with per-rank-channel hyperparameters, and only without global-norm clipping. At $\varepsilon=0$ and without weight decay it suffices to rescale the rates, $\eta_{B,j}\mapsto\eta_{B,j}/d_j$ and $\eta_{A,j}\mapsto d_j\eta_{A,j}$. With $\varepsilon>0$ one must also give each channel its own $\varepsilon_{B,j}=d_j\varepsilon$ and $\varepsilon_{A,j}=\varepsilon/d_j$. With decoupled decay multiplied by the learning rate (PyTorch AdamW) one must also set $\lambda_{B,j}=d_j\lambda$ and $\lambda_{A,j}=\lambda/d_j$; under Loshchilov–Hutter's convention, where decay is scaled only by the schedule multiplier, $\lambda$ needs no change.

(c) Undamped scaled GD ($\delta=0$; the Riemannian preconditioner of Tong–Ma–Chi and Zhang–Pilanci, with or without heavy-ball momentum): all of $GL_r$. The $W$-trajectory from gauge-related initial states (with zero or correspondingly transformed momentum) is independent of the representative; a step may drop rank, so the iteration maps into $W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}$.

*Published variants.* Damped scaled GD ($\delta>0$) is only $O(r)$-equivariant, and Zhang–Pilanci's scaled AdamW, their practical variant, is $B_r$-equivariant by (b). LoRA-Pro's $W$-level first-order dynamics descend (Prop III.4(b)), as does its variant with free matrix $X=0$; its published parameter update with the Sylvester-optimal $X$ is only $O(r)$-equivariant, and its discrete $W$-step depends on the representative at $O(\eta^2)$. LoRA-RITE states only $W$-level invariance (its Definition 1). Its update (unmagnified gradients, transported matrix moments, then multiplication by $(R_B^\top)^+$) is $GL_r$-equivariant at the parameter level on the full-rank locus: under a gauge change its internal quantities change only by orthogonal matrices, and the final multiplication supplies the gauge element (the proof follows its Algorithm 1 line by line; the resulting step is Eq. 15 of the LoRA-RITE paper). It is therefore an Adam-type optimizer in class (c).

*Proof.* Under $(Bg^{-1},gA)$ we have $\nabla_{B'}=\nabla_Bg^\top$ and $\nabla_{A'}=g^{-\top}\nabla_A$. Equivariance means $\delta B'=\delta Bg^{-1}$ and $\delta A'=g\,\delta A$, for arbitrary gradients.

(a) SGD needs $g^\top=g^{-1}$, and orthogonal $g$ suffice. A heavy-ball or Nesterov buffer is a linear combination of past gradients at points related by the same $g$, so for orthogonal $g$ it transforms like the gradients and the step is transported. The first step from a zero buffer is a positive multiple of an SGD step ($1$ for heavy ball, $1+\mu_m$ for Nesterov with momentum coefficient $\mu_m$), so the inclusion $\subseteq O(r)$ also holds with momentum.

(b) "$\supseteq$": coordinatewise rules commute with signed permutations, since the moment $m$ flips sign and $v$ does not change.

"$\subseteq$": the first Adam step from zero state is $\delta B=-\eta\,\phi(\nabla_B)$ with $\phi(x)=x/(\lvert x\rvert+\varepsilon)$ coordinatewise. Write $h=g^{-1}$.
- *Case $\varepsilon=0$.* Equivariance reads $\mathrm{sign}(xg^\top)=\mathrm{sign}(x)h$ for every row vector $x$. Every sign pattern $s\in\{\pm1\}^r$ is realised by some $x$ with $xg^\top$ zero-free, since the open orthant of $s$ is not covered by the proper hyperplanes $(xg^\top)_j=0$ (a finite union of hyperplanes has empty interior), so $sh\in\{\pm1\}^r$ for all $s$. Flipping $s_i$ changes $(sh)_j$ by $2s_ih_{ij}\in\{0,\pm2\}$, so $h_{ij}\in\{0,\pm1\}$. A column with $t\ne1$ nonzero entries yields a sum of absolute value $0$ or $\ge2$ for suitable $s$, so each column of $h$ has exactly one entry $\pm1$. Hence $h$, and therefore $g$, lies in $B_r$.
- *Case $\varepsilon>0$.* For small $x$, $\phi(x)\approx x/\varepsilon$, which forces $g\in O(r)$. For large $x$, $\phi\to\mathrm{sign}$, which forces $g\in B_r$.

For the joint torus: with $g=\mathrm{diag}(d)$, gradient column $j$ of $B$ is scaled by $d_j$, while equivariance requires the step to be scaled by $1/d_j$. At $\varepsilon=0$, Adam's step is invariant to positive per-coordinate scaling of the gradient, so the rate of column $j$ must be multiplied by $1/d_j$, and the rate of row $j$ of $A$ by $d_j$. With $\varepsilon>0$, $d_jm/(d_j\sqrt v+\varepsilon')=m/(\sqrt v+\varepsilon'/d_j)$, which requires $\varepsilon'=d_j\varepsilon$; the decoupled decay term $\eta'\lambda'B'_{:,j}=(\eta/d_j)\lambda'B_{:,j}/d_j$ requires $\lambda'=d_j\lambda$.
