- *B:* exact-Cayley block OFT is idempotent up to closure. Iterated BOFT reaches $\mathrm{diag}\cdot W_0\cdot P\big(\prod SO(b\,2^{m'-1})\big)P^\top$ for a fixed permutation $P$, and, for injective $W_0$, $\mathrm{diag}\cdot W_0\cdot SO(n)$ only at full butterfly depth.
- *M:* fuses.

**(IA)$^3$.** $\mathrm{diag}(\ell)W_0$ on $k$ and $v$; $W_0\mathrm{diag}(\ell)$ on $W_{\rm down}$.
- *I:* linear, the closure of a torus orbit. Zeros of $W_0$ stay zero, and row (resp. column) lines are fixed while $\ell_i\ne0$. It lies in $\mathrm{HiRA}_1$, with covariance $\mathrm{Mon}\times GL$ on output-scaled boxes and $GL\times\mathrm{Mon}$ on $W_{\rm down}$.
- *B:* idempotent.
- *M:* fuses into $W_K$, $W_V$ and $W_{\rm down}$; alternatively into $W_{\rm up}$ under SwiGLU, but not under GELU.

**VeRA.** $W_0+\Lambda_bB\Lambda_dA$, with $B,A$ frozen and shared, and $b_0=0$.
- *I:* a toric cone of dimension $m+r-1$, with apex at $\theta_0$; to first order only $b$ moves.
- *F:* gauge $\mathbb R^\times$, charge $\|b\|^2/\eta_b-\|d\|^2/\eta_d$.
- *B:* $\mathrm{VeRA}^\infty=$ LoRA-FA$(A)$. There is an arrow VeRA $\to$ LoRA-FA$(A)$.

**LoHa / LoKr.**
- LoHa$_{r,r}$ is a Hadamard variety of rank $\le\min(r^2,m,n)$ and dimension $\min\big(mn,\,2r(m+n)-2r^2-(m+n-1)\big)$, with gauge $GL_r^2\times$ diagonal torus and an apex at the zero factor. For $r\le2$ and $2r^2<m+n-1$ it is strictly dominated by LoRA$_{2r}$.
- LoKr $C\otimes BA$ is $\mathcal R^{-1}_*$ of a constrained LoRA$_1$. With inner rank $r'\le\min(m_2,n_2)$ its image lies in LoRA$_{\min(m_1,n_1)r'}$, and when $\kappa_1>1$ it is incomparable with zero-init LoRA$_r$ iff $r<\min(m_1,n_1)r'$ (Thm II.11(e)).

**FourierFT.** $W_0+\alpha\,\mathrm{Re}F^{-1}(\text{sparse }c)$.
- *I:* a coordinate mask in the real cosine frame, inside the point-symmetric subspace; linear and regular. Conjugate pairs among the sampled frequencies add a gauge. HF's default random initialisation leaves a small defect.
- *B:* idempotent with fixed indices.
- Its covariance is $\Pi_{\rm cyc}$, so a neuron permutation changes it.

**BitFit.** A linear translation of the bias block.
- *I:* regular.
- *B:* idempotent.
- *M:* nothing to merge.

**Adapters.** An extension $x\mapsto x+U\sigma(Dx)$ with neutral point $U=0$ (zero-up initialisation; Houlsby's own near-identity init leaves a small defect).
- *F:* the gauge is permutations, plus per-unit rescalings under ReLU. At $U=0$ only $U$ moves to first order (Lemma II.4, function-level variant).
- *M:* not box-mergeable; with $\sigma=\mathrm{id}$, an adapter parallel to a single linear box is LoRA, while one parallel to a whole sublayer edits the skip connection and stays M∞.

**Prefix / prompt tuning.**
- Both are unpointed extensions on the sequence wire, in the sense of Thm VI.5(f): on a head with $W_oW_v\ne0$ no prefix is neutral.
- Prefix tuning is a gated parallel adapter on attention, with rigid content ratios at the layer where it acts.
- Prompt tuning maps into deep prefix tuning in causal decoders.
- LLaMA-Adapter supplies the missing neutral point.
- *M:* not mergeable.

**PiSSA / LoftQ / QLoRA.**
- PiSSA is a Type II object: the cone translated to vertex $W_0-[W_0]_r$, with $\theta_0$ a smooth point. It is balanced at equal rates.
- QLoRA is LoRA pointed at $\kappa\theta_0$, with defect $e$.
- LoftQ with one iteration quantises first and fits the error, leaving $e$ minus its best rank-$r$ approximation when the LoRA scale is $1$; HF's LoftQ ignores the scale, so with $\alpha=2r$ the defect is as large as $e$. QPiSSA splits first. The order of operations matters.
- *M:* exact merging leaves the quantised type; type-preserving merges are lossy.

**ReLoRA.** Rebased LoRA. It reaches exactly $\mathcal M_{\le Kr}$, and full fine-tuning at $K^*=\lceil\min(m,n)/r\rceil$. It is $\mathrm{LoRA}_r^{\oplus K}$ unrolled in time.

**GaLore.** A backward lens with compression $P_t^\top$ and anchor $P_t$. Within a period it is one-sided LoRA; across periods it carries optimizer state over without transporting it. Its anchor is constant on each period, so it is trivially integrable, including at $T=1$; what distinguishes it from LoRA is the resampling schedule plus the state carry-over.

**Merging.**
- Task arithmetic is $GL(\Theta)$-natural. TIES is natural under homotheties times signed permutations, and DARE under the monomial group (the torus exactly, permutations in law); neither under rotations.
- Post-hoc factor-wise rules (LoraHub, HF's `linear`) are gauge-dependent. Joint frames that produce a shared factor, or a shared frozen $A$, fix them.
- Merging an adapter into the base is the canonical map to the terminal object.

---

## Exposé VIII. The classification

### VIII.A Seven axes, each decidable from the formula

Full definitions and examples are in `theory/axes.json`.
1. **Kind.** R (reparametrisation) · E (pointed extension) · E° (unpointed extension) · B (backward lens or schedule) · P (post-hoc operation) · F$_T$ (family over tasks, inputs or tenants).
2. **Shape** of the image germ. Lin ($\rho$ affine) · Cone (multihomogeneous, non-linear) · Cone* (translated cone, split pointing) · Orb (group orbit) · Sat (group × cone) · Meet (orbit ∩ cone) · Fun (function-level).
3. **Base point**, read from $S_1$. regular · apex (deficit $>0$) · neutral · defect ($\rho(q_0)\neq\theta_0$) · unpointed.
4. **Gauge**, from wire insertions and spider slides, checked by Jacobian rank. none · scalar · torus · $GL$ · $GL\times$torus · non-linear.
5. **Covariance group** (the Klein geometry of the image). $GL\times GL$ · $CO\times CO$ · $GL\times CO$ · $\mathrm{Mon}\times GL$ · $\mathrm{Mon}\times\mathrm{Mon}$ · $\Pi_X$ (imposed structure) · frame (random or data, in law).
6. **Merge type.** M1 (into its box) · M1→ (into a neighbour via R1–R7, under side conditions) · Mq (an exact merge leaves the quantised type) · M∞ (not box-locally mergeable).
7. **Rebasing closure.** idempotent · →span · →group · schedule.

*Conventions.* "–" (null in `axes.json` and in the classified data) means that the axis does not apply, and no axis has a separate "not applicable" value; the merge axis's legacy key `na`, used for backward and schedule methods, means the same. Shape and gauge need a parameter space, so they are "–" for closed-form post-hoc operations with no tunable coefficient. Rebasing needs a weight-space merge, so it is "–" for every M∞ method. Covariance applies to every method with a formula; for function-level methods it is the function-level analogue of Def II.14, routed mixtures included. Entries without an exact key are coded to the nearest key, as listed in `axes.json`.


### VIII.B The periodic table

Notation: $d$ is the expressive dimension, $\rho_{\max}$ the maximum rank, and $k$ (in GraLoRA) the number of blocks per side. "Π" means imposed structure, "fr" a random or data frame. Covariance is written (output side) × (input side).

| method | kind | shape ($d$; $\rho_{\max}$) | base pt | gauge | covariance | merge | rebasing |

† BOFT's identity is regular when the factors' plane sets are disjoint (e.g. $b=2$), because then the factor Lie algebras form a direct sum. "≈" marks implementations whose default initialisation is near, but not exactly at, $\theta_0$: FourierFT and C3A defaults in HF PEFT, VB-LoRA, Uni-LoRA, TinyLoRA, and LoRA-XS ($R_0\sim\mathcal N(0,10^{-10})$). They are objects of the unpointed slice with a pointing defect, small for LoRA-XS and TinyLoRA but not for HF's FourierFT ($\mathcal N(0,1)$ spectrum with scaling $150$, about $14\%$ of a std-$0.04$ $W_0$ at $768^2$ with $1000$ frequencies), and the table classifies their exactly pointed variants. "$CO\times CO$": frames built from truncated SVDs are equivariant under positive scalings as well as orthogonal maps, so the spectral-frame geometry is $CO(m)\times CO(n)$ (Prop II.16); bare singular frames used without their singular values (LoRA-GA) are scale-invariant rather than covariant, which leaves $O(m)\times O(n)$. "(fn.)" marks the function-level analogue of Def II.14: the inserted or generated family on each adapted wire is invariant under conjugation by $(g,h)$ on that wire. "–" means the axis does not apply (null in `theory/axes.json` and in the classified data). Where the classified data refines an entry (Kind, Gauge, Covariance and Merge of LST, UniPELT/MAM, MPT, Compacter, Houlsby, DeLoRA, AdaLoRA, LoRETTA, LoRA-SB, Trainable Tokens, VB-LoRA, LISA, HyperFormer, S-LoRA/Punica), the table follows the data.

### VIII.C Arrow rules: when $N$ simulates $M$

A morphism $M\to N$ is exhibited by an explicit pointed $h$ with $\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M$. The rules below generate every arrow in the atlas.
- **A1, interval.** Frozen $\to M\to$ Full FT, with $h=\mathrm{const}_{q_0}$ and $h=\rho_M$ respectively.
- **A2, freezing gives sub-objects.** Freezing coordinates at their base values gives a monomorphism $h(q')=(q',q_0'')$. Examples: LoRA-FA$(A_0)\hookrightarrow$ LoRA$_{(0,A_0)}$, FB$(B_0)\hookrightarrow$ LoRA, MiCA $\hookrightarrow$ LoRA$_{(B_0,0)}$.
- **A3, factorisation through a polynomial map.** Examples:
  - VeRA $\to$ LoRA-FA$(A)$, $h=(\Lambda_bB\Lambda_d)$;
  - NOLA $\to$ LoRA$_r$, $h=(\sum\alpha_iB_i,\sum\beta_jA_j)$;
  - LoHa$_{r_1,r_2}\to$ LoRA$_{r_1r_2}$, by the face-splitting product $h=(B_1\bullet B_2,(A_1^\top\bullet A_2^\top)^\top)$;
  - LoRA$_r\hookrightarrow$ LoRA$_{r+1}$, $h=([B\ 0],[A;a_0])$;
  - DyLoRA$_k\hookrightarrow$ DyLoRA$_{k+1}$;
  - LoRA-XS $\to$ PiSSA, $h(R)=(U_r(S_r+R)S_r^{-1/2},S_r^{1/2}V_r^\top)$;
  - HRA$_r\to$ LoRA$_r$ pointed at $h(q_0)$, via $R-I=\sum_iH_1\cdots H_{i-1}(H_i-I)$;
  - (IA)$^3\to$ HiRA$_1$ pointed at $(0,\mathbf 1^\top)$, $h(\ell)=(\ell-\mathbf 1,\mathbf 1^\top)$;
  - isomorphisms: MiSS $\cong$ LoRA-FA$(\mathbf 1^\top\otimes I_r)$; rsLoRA $\cong$ LoRA, $h=(\sqrt rB,A)$; linear adapter parallel to one linear box $\cong$ LoRA.
- **A4, products and meets.** A product comes with two projections. Examples: LoRA-XS $\to$ FA$(A_0)$ and LoRA-XS $\to$ FB$(B_0)$ at the level of objects; HRA $\to$ OFT$_{SO}$ and HRA $\to$ LoRA$_r$ at the level of images only.
- **A5, sums.** $M\to M\oplus N$, $q\mapsto(q,q_0^N)$. Also LoRA$_r\oplus$LoRA$_s\cong$ LoRA$_{r+s}$, unpointed (Obs I.7). Rebasing stages give $M^{\oplus K}\to M^{\oplus(K+1)}$.
- **Transport is not an arrow.** $T_*$ for $T\in GL(\Theta)$ is an automorphism of the slice (Prop II.13). Methods related by transport, such as the Kronecker family and LoRA (across $\Theta'\cong\Theta$), are "the same object in another frame". Transports that depend on $\theta_0$ (HiRA, SVD frames) agree with their source only at the base point.
- **Obstructions.** There is no arrow $M\to N$ if any of the following holds:
  - $\mathrm{Im}M\not\subseteq\mathrm{Im}N$, for instance because $N$ preserves an absolute invariant that $M$ breaks;
  - $d(M)>d(N)$;
  - $\rho_{\max}(M)>\rho_{\max}(N)$;
  - $S_1(M)\not\subseteq S_1(N)$;
  - $M$ and $N$ lie in different strata of Thm II.2 (Type I vs Type II).

---

## Exposé IX. Rosetta stone

This table gives the core entries. The full dictionary is in `theory/rosetta.json`.

| PEFT | categorical / geometric | where |
|---|---|---|
| pretrained checkpoint | base point; pointed 1-cell of $\mathbf{Para}$ | I.1 |
| PEFT method | object of $\mathbf{Diff}_*/(\Theta,\theta_0)$, the opposite of the coslice of 2-cells under $F$ | I.2–I.3 |
| "$N$ can do whatever $M$ can" | morphism (simulation) | I.2 |
| frozen / full fine-tuning; merging | initial / terminal object; canonical map to the terminal object | I.4 |
| zero-init vs split init | apex vs smooth point; Type I vs Type II | II.2, III.8 |
| initialisation schemes | points of three strata, $\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\sqcup\mathrm{Gr}(r,m)\sqcup\mathcal M_r$ (full-rank pointings, set-level) | II.2 |
| rank vs parameter count | min-cut vs volume | II.10–II.12 |
| "new method = old one" | transport along $GL(\Theta)$, an automorphism of the slice | II.13 |
| HRA; LoRA-XS | image-level meet; categorical product | II.7, I.8 |
| balancedness; LR ratio and $\alpha$ | Noether charge of $\mathrm{Sym}_r$; torus orbit space | III.5, III.10 |
| invariant optimizer | equivariance under the whole gauge; descent | III.12 |
| GaLore vs LoRA | cotangent vs tangent lens, equal at degree one (weight decay $0$, optimizer state carried over) | IV.2 |
| QLoRA / LoftQ | base change; pointing defect (three orders of operations); approximate section | V.2 |
| adapters, prompts; zero latency | pointed / unpointed extensions; box-local lift by rewriting under side conditions | VI |

## Exposé X. Falsifiable predictions

Each prediction carries its optimizer scope.
1. **LoRA+ vs re-tuned $\alpha$** (Adam). The two coincide unless the torus is broken by global-norm clipping, by $\varepsilon$ or weight decay shared across groups, or by group-specific schedules (Thm III.10, Cor III.11).
2. **DoRA vs LoRA + output scale** (AdamW; an experiment rather than a prediction). Their images are equal (Prop II.8), so a gap at the same initialisation can only come from the parametrisation, the backward rule or the optimizer coordinates. Experiment D's four arms separate these; we make no claim yet about which arm closes the gap.
3. **Permutation test.** Over function-preserving hidden-unit permutations, LoRA's outcome variance equals its seed variance; FourierFT, LoKr, MoRA, and BOFT on `down_proj` show larger variance (Lemma VI.2).
4. **Rotation test.** A single global residual rotation of a gain-fused pre-norm model provably leaves the reachable function sets of LoRA, (IA)$^3$ (HF defaults) and DoRA on input-rotated boxes unchanged (Obs II.15); the control is the fused but unrotated model. Training outcomes are exactly unchanged only for (IA)$^3$, and in law for LoRA and DoRA under SGD with an isotropic initialisation; HF's Kaiming-uniform init and AdamW break this. The rotation is conjectured to change the reachable sets of block OFT/BOFT, LoHa ($r\ge2$), HiRA and masks (Conj A).
5. **Rank-one targets.** With a loss whose only minimiser is the target, LoRA$_1$ fits them; Kronecker sums with fewer than $\kappa_1=\min(m_1,m_2)\min(n_1,n_2)$ terms cannot ($\kappa_1=N$ for equal square $N\times N$ splits, such as HF's $64\times64$ split of $4096$; Thm II.11).
6. **Re-OFT.** With an exact Cayley map, fixed blocks gain nothing, while partitions whose hypergraph is connected generate $SO(n)$ (Thm V.3).
7. **Crossover** (SGD). Singular values of $\Delta W$ turn two-sided near $\sigma^*$, within a band of relative width $O(\sqrt{r/n})$ for Kaiming initialisations (Thm III.9).
8. **Charge drift.** $\Phi$ is flat under gradient flow, its rescaling $\eta\Phi$ drifts by $O(\eta^2)$ per SGD step, and it drifts under Adam (Thm III.5).
9. **LoraHub seeds.** Independent $A$-seeds degrade factor-wise composition (Prop V.4).
10. **Effective dimension.** Ranking at equal $d(M)$ changes small-budget leaderboards (Prop II.12).
11. **Whitened splits.** These are more robust to function-preserving input reparametrisations (Prop II.16, Conj C).

---

## Exposé XI. What is analogy, and what is open

**Analogy, stated plainly.**
- *"Noether"* names the pattern from symmetry to charge. The law itself is proved directly for gradient flow (Thm III.5, after Zhao et al. 2023): discrete steps drift by $O(\eta^2)$ relative to the charge, and Adam and momentum break it.
- *"Erlangen"* is a theorem for group-type methods. For cones it is a naming device, and the content is the shape and its covariance.
- *"Gauge"* means the symmetry group of a parametrisation. There is no connection, and away from the full-rank stratum the fibres jump.
- *"Moduli space"* means a set of isomorphism classes. Ours are set-level classifications of distinguished strata; the natural quotient topology is not a disjoint union, and no stack is built.
- The *lens duality* is exact only at degree one, and only for optimizers that do not read the parameter tensor, with no weight decay.
- *Expressivity is not learnability.* Only Exposé III is dynamical, and it states its scope.
- *Mergeability* is proved box-locally only, under explicit side conditions (position-uniformity, no weight sharing, bias slots); the rewrite rules give sufficient conditions, not a decision procedure.

**Open problems.**
1. Prove the LoHa dimension formula in general, and decide whether the Hadamard variety is closed.
2. Classify initialisations dynamically: the apex fibre modulo $B_r$ (Adam), stratified by $\Phi_0$, including the degenerate pointings outside the three strata of Thm II.2.
3. Is HRA's apex a practical first-order bottleneck? Compare against a regular initialisation at matched budget.
4. When is a meet of two named methods parametrisable by a polynomial chart, as HRA's image and LoRA-XS are?
5. Find the size-$k$ frontier of $\mathsf{Meth}$, whose maximal elements are pairwise incomparable (Thm II.11).
6. Find an Adam analogue of Thm III.9: a conserved or monotone quantity for sign-like updates.
7. For a vanilla softmax head with $W_o\ne0$ and $W_q\ne0$, a generic prefix is not box-locally mergeable (Thm VI.5(f)); prove or refute its non-mergeability for the whole network, and prove the generic strictness of prompt tuning inside P-tuning v2 that Petrov–Torr–Bibi and Li–Liang assert (it holds in a one-layer, one-token example).
